(89)
where the minℓ,t\min\limits_{\ell,t} or maxℓ,t\max\limits_{\ell,t} are taken over all sub-blocks ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m] and all token indices t∈ℐℓYt\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}.
With Lemma C.1 in place, we then derive the asymptotic joint distribution of (U,π⁡(w1),…,π⁡(wm))(U,\pi(w_{1}),\ldots,\pi(w_{m})) when |𝒲|→∞|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty.
To do so, we examine the limiting expectation 𝔼⁡[J⁡(U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))]\EB\left[J(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\ldots,\eta(\pi(w_{m})))\right] for any arbitrary test function JJ.
Theorem C.1 (Asymptotic distribution under H1H_{1}).
Let ℐζ{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta} be a minimal unit consisting of mm sub-blocks {ℐℓY}ℓ=1m\{{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m}, and let {wℓ}ℓ=1m⊆𝒲\{w_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m}\subseteq{{\mathcal{W}}} denote the distinct tokens representing these sub-blocks.
Let Assumptions 3.1 and 5.1 hold.
Let (Δt)t∈ℐζ(\Delta_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}} be the per-time regularity levels, where each Δt∈[Δ,1−δ]\Delta_{t}\in[\Delta,1-\delta], and define the sub-block regularity vector (Δ¯1,…,Δ¯m)({\bar{\Delta}}_{1},\ldots,{\bar{\Delta}}_{m}) by Δ¯ℓ:=maxt∈ℐℓY⁡Δt.{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}:=\max_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\Delta_{t}.
Then for any measurable function J:[0,1]m+1→[0,∞)J\colon[0,1]^{m+1}\to[0,\infty), the expectation in Corollary C.1 converges as |𝒲|→∞|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty to
lim|𝒲|→∞𝔼1,𝐏ℐζ⁡[J⁡(U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))]\displaystyle\lim_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty}\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}}}\bigl[J(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\dots,\eta(\pi(w_{m})))\bigr]
=1Im​(𝚫¯)∫[0,1]m∫maxℓ∈[m]⁡Δ¯ℓ​xℓminℓ∈[m]⁡(1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓ​xℓ)J(u,x1,…,xm)𝟏{minℓ∈[m](1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓxℓ)≥maxℓ∈[m]Δ¯ℓxℓ}dudx1⋯dxm,\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}})}\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\int_{\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}}^{\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}(1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell})}J(u,x_{1},\dots,x_{m}){\bf 1}_{\left\{\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}(1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell})\geq\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}\right\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}u{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m},
(90)
where the normalization constant Im​(𝚫¯)I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}) is the volume of the integration region:
Im(𝚫¯):=∫[0,1]m(minℓ∈[m](1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓxℓ)−maxℓ∈[m]Δ¯ℓxℓ)+dx1⋯dxm.I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}):=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\left(\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}(1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell})-\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}\right)_{+}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m}.
Moreover, the convergence holds uniformly over any 1-Lipschitz test functions JJ, any NTP distributions 𝐏ℐζ\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}} within the class 𝒬τ,𝚫{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}}, and any regularity vectors Δ¯\bar{{\Delta}}.
The proof of Theorem C.1 can be found in Section C.5.
The arbitrariness of the test function JJ in Theorem C.1 directly implies the following weak convergence.
Corollary C.2 (Asymptotic distribution under H1H_{1}).
Under the same notation and assumptions as in Theorem C.1, the joint vector
(U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\ldots,\eta(\pi(w_{m})))
converges in distribution to a random vector (U,X1,…,Xm)(U,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}), where X1,…,XmX_{1},\ldots,X_{m} are i.i.d. Unif⁡(0,1)\mathrm{Unif}(0,1) random variables, and UU is independently drawn from the interval
[maxℓ∈[m]⁡{Δ¯ℓ​Xℓ},minℓ∈[m]⁡{1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓ​Xℓ}]\left[\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}\{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}X_{\ell}\},\ \min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}\{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}X_{\ell}\}\right]
conditioned on the event that this interval is non-empty, that is, maxℓ∈[m]⁡{Δ¯ℓ​Xℓ}≤minℓ∈[m]⁡{1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓ​Xℓ}\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}\{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}X_{\ell}\}\leq\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}\{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}X_{\ell}\}.
Proof of Lemma C.2.
This follows directly from Theorem C.1, together with the Portmanteau theorem (Theorem 13.16 in [23]) and Lemma C.9, which together allow us to translate convergence in expectation into weak convergence.
∎
By an argument similar to that of Theorem C.1, we can show that
Lemma C.2 (Asymptotic distribution under H0H_{0}).
Under the null hypothesis H0H_{0}, the joint distribution of (U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\ldots,\eta(\pi(w_{m}))) converges weakly to that of (U,X1,…,Xm)(U,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}), where U,X1,…,XmU,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m} are i.i.d. Unif⁡(0,1)\mathrm{Unif}(0,1).
∎
C.2 Proof of Theorem 5.1
Proof of Theorem 5.1.
This theorem establishes the asymptotic joint distribution of the pivotal statistics within a minimal unit. To achieve this, we will make use of the results in Lemma 5.1.
Null joint distribution of pivotal statistics.
We first derive the joint distribution under H0H_{0} for the pivotal statistics (Yt)t∈ℐζ(Y_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}} within a minimal unit.
By Lemma 5.1, as |𝒲|→∞|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty, each Yℓ=|U−η⁡(π⁡(wℓ))|Y_{\ell}=|U-\eta(\pi(w_{\ell}))| converges weakly to |U−Xℓ||U-X_{\ell}| under H0H_{0}, where U,X1,…,XmU,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m} are i.i.d. random variables uniformly distributed on [0,1][0,1].
With a slight abuse of notation, we relabel Yℓ:=|U−Xℓ|Y_{\ell}:=|U-X_{\ell}| for ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m] to simplify notation.
We begin by analyzing the conditional CDF of YℓY_{\ell} given U=uU=u.
Fix u∈[0,1]u\in[0,1] and take any y∈[0,1]y\in[0,1]. The conditional CDF of YℓY_{\ell} is:
ℙ⁡(Yℓ≤y∣U=u)\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{\ell}\leq y\mid U=u)
=ℙ⁡(|U−Xℓ|≤y∣U=u)\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(|U-X_{\ell}|\leq y\mid U=u)
(91)
=ℙ⁡(u−y≤Xℓ≤u+y∣U=u)\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(u-y\leq X_{\ell}\leq u+y\mid U=u)
(92)
=[(u+y)∧1]−[(u−y)∨0].\displaystyle=[(u+y)\wedge 1]-[(u-y)\vee 0].
(93)
The corresponding conditional PDF is then
fYℓ|U​(y∣u)={2,if ​0<y<min⁡(u,1−u),1,if ​min⁡(u,1−u)<y<max⁡(u,1−u),0,otherwise.\displaystyle f_{Y_{\ell}\mid U}(y\mid u)=\begin{cases}2,&\text{if }0<y<\min(u,1-u),\\
1,&\text{if }\min(u,1-u)<y<\max(u,1-u),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
(94)
Consequently, under H0H_{0}, the joint PDF of 𝒀=(Y1,…,Ym)\bm{Y}=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}) given U=uU=u is:
f𝒀​(y1,…,ym)=∫01fY1,…,Ym|U​(y1,…,ym∣u)​𝑑u=∫01∏ℓ=1mfYℓ|U​(yℓ∣u)​𝑑u.\displaystyle f_{\bm{Y}}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})=\int_{0}^{1}f_{Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}\mid U}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}\mid u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u=\int_{0}^{1}\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}f_{Y_{\ell}\mid U}(y_{\ell}\mid u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u.
(95)
To simplify this expression, define two index sets depending on uu:
I1​(u)\displaystyle I_{1}(u)
={ℓ:0<yℓ<min⁡(u,1−u)},\displaystyle=\{\ell:0<y_{\ell}<\min(u,1-u)\},
(96)
I2​(u)\displaystyle I_{2}(u)
={ℓ:yℓ≥max⁡(u,1−u)}.\displaystyle=\{\ell:y_{\ell}\geq\max(u,1-u)\}.
(97)
Then the joint density becomes:
f0​(y1,…,ym):=f𝒀​(y1,…,ym)=∫012|I1​(u)|​𝟏I2​(u)=∅​𝑑u.\displaystyle f_{0}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}):=f_{\bm{Y}}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})=\int_{0}^{1}2^{|I_{1}(u)|}{\bf 1}_{I_{2}(u)=\emptyset}\,{\mathrm{d}}u.
(98)
We will later provide an alternative representation of this density that is more convenient for theoretical analysis but more complex in form. For numerical computations, however, the integral expression in (98) is preferable.
Alternative joint distribution of pivotal statistics.
We now derive the joint distribution under H1H_{1} for the pivotal statistics (Yt)t∈ℐζ(Y_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}} within a minimal unit. According to Lemma 5.1, under H1H_{1}, the tuple (U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\ldots,\eta(\pi(w_{m}))) converges in distribution to (U,X1,…,Xm)(U,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}), where X1,…,XmX_{1},\ldots,X_{m} are i.i.d. Unif⁡(0,1)\mathrm{Unif}(0,1) random variables, and UU is drawn independently and uniformly from the interval
[maxℓ∈[m]⁡{Δ¯ℓ​Xℓ},minℓ∈[m]⁡{1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓ​Xℓ}],\left[\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}\{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}X_{\ell}\},\ \min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}\{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}X_{\ell}\}\right],
conditioned on the interval being non-empty. For convenience, we denote Yℓ:=|U−Xℓ|Y_{\ell}:=|U-X_{\ell}| for ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m].
To obtain the joint density of (Y1,…,Ym)(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}), we consider the transformation
Φ:(U,X1,…,Xm)↦(U,Y1,…,Ym)=(U,|U−X1|,…,|U−Xm|).\Phi:(U,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})\mapsto(U,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})=(U,|U-X_{1}|,\ldots,|U-X_{m}|).
Since Φ\Phi is continuous and the joint law of (U,X1,…,Xm)(U,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}) is absolutely continuous, we may ignore boundary events (e.g., Yℓ=0Y_{\ell}=0 for some ℓ\ell) which have zero measure.
However, Φ\Phi is not injective due to the absolute values. To apply the change-of-variable formula, we partition the domain into disjoint regions where Φ\Phi becomes bijective. For each sign vector 𝝈=(σ1,…,σm)∈{−1,1}m\bm{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{m})\in\{-1,1\}^{m}, define the region
ℛ𝝈:={(u,x1,…,xm)∈[0,1]m+1:sign⁡(xℓ−u)=σℓ​ for all ​ℓ}.\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\bm{\sigma}}:=\left\{(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m+1}:\sign(x_{\ell}-u)=\sigma_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\right\}.
(99)
Within ℛ𝝈\mathcal{R}_{\bm{\sigma}}, we have xℓ=u+σℓ​yℓx_{\ell}=u+\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell} and Φ\Phi is bijective with Jacobian determinant of absolute value 1. Thus, the joint density of (U,Y1,…,Ym)(U,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}) on this region is directly given by the density of (U,X1,…,Xm)(U,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}) evaluated at (u,x1,…,xm)=(u,u+σ1​y1,…,u+σm​ym)(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})=(u,u+\sigma_{1}y_{1},\ldots,u+\sigma_{m}y_{m}).
To integrate out the nuisance parameter UU, we first characterize the feasible values of uu given 𝒚=(y1,…,ym)\bm{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}) and a fixed sign vector 𝝈=(σ1,…,σm)\bm{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{m}). The first requirement is that each reconstructed xℓ=u+σℓ​yℓx_{\ell}=u+\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell} must lie within the unit interval [0,1][0,1], which leads to the constraint:
L𝝈​(𝒚):=maxℓ⁡(−σℓ​yℓ)≤u≤minℓ⁡(1−σℓ​yℓ)=:U𝝈​(𝒚).\displaystyle L_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y}):=\max_{\ell}(-\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell})\leq u\leq\min_{\ell}(1-\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell})=:U_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y}).
Second, we enforce the conditional event to hold from Corollary C.1, which requires
Δ¯ℓ​xℓ≤u≤1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓ​xℓfor all ​ℓ.{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}\leq u\leq 1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}\quad\text{for all }\ell.
Substituting xℓ=u+σℓ​yℓx_{\ell}=u+\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell} and solving for uu leads to
Δ¯ℓ​σℓ​yℓ1−Δ¯ℓ≤u≤1+Δ¯ℓ​σℓ​yℓ1−Δ¯ℓ.\frac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}\leq u\leq 1+\frac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}.
Taking the maximum lower bound and minimum upper bound across ℓ\ell, we define
Y𝝈+​(𝒚)\displaystyle Y_{\bm{\sigma}}^{+}(\bm{y})
:=max⁡({Δ¯ℓ​yℓ1−Δ¯ℓ:σℓ=+1}∪{0}),\displaystyle:=\max\left(\left\{\frac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}y_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}:\sigma_{\ell}=+1\right\}\cup\{0\}\right),
Y𝝈−​(𝒚)\displaystyle Y_{\bm{\sigma}}^{-}(\bm{y})
:=max⁡({Δ¯ℓ​yℓ1−Δ¯ℓ:σℓ=−1}∪{0}),\displaystyle:=\max\left(\left\{\frac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}y_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}:\sigma_{\ell}=-1\right\}\cup\{0\}\right),
which yield the additional constraint:
Y𝝈+​(𝒚)≤u≤1−Y𝝈−​(𝒚).Y_{\bm{\sigma}}^{+}(\bm{y})\leq u\leq 1-Y_{\bm{\sigma}}^{-}(\bm{y}).
Combining both sets of constraints, the overall feasible range for uu is the interval
[A𝝈𝚫¯(𝒚),B𝝈𝚫¯(𝒚)],where{A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚):=max⁡{L𝝈​(𝒚),Y𝝈+​(𝒚)},B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚):=min⁡{U𝝈​(𝒚),1−Y𝝈−​(𝒚)}.\left[A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y}),B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y})\right],\quad\text{where}\quad\begin{cases}A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y}):=\max\{L_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y}),Y_{\bm{\sigma}}^{+}(\bm{y})\},\\
B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y}):=\min\{U_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y}),1-Y_{\bm{\sigma}}^{-}(\bm{y})\}.\end{cases}
Since the density of (U,X1,…,Xm)(U,X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}) is constant and equals 1/Im​(𝚫¯)1/I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}) over its support, the contribution from each region is proportional to the length of this feasible interval:
ℓ𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚):=(B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)−A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚))+.\ell_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y}):=\left(B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y})-A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y})\right)_{+}.
Summing over all 2m2^{m} sign vectors, the joint density of 𝒀=(Y1,…,Ym)\bm{Y}=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}) is
f𝚫¯​(𝒚):=f𝒀𝚫¯​(𝒚)=1Im​(𝚫¯)​∑𝝈∈{−1,1}mℓ𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚).f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y}):=f_{\bm{Y}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y})=\frac{1}{I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}})}\sum_{\bm{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{m}}\ell_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\bm{y}).
Remark C.1.
As a sanity check, when 𝚫¯=𝟎\bar{\bm{\Delta}}={\bf 0}, we recover the null case. In that case, Y𝝈+=Y𝝈−=0Y_{\bm{\sigma}}^{+}=Y_{\bm{\sigma}}^{-}=0, and the constraint reduces to L𝝈​(𝒚)≤u≤U𝝈​(𝒚)L_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\leq u\leq U_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y}), matching the joint density under H0H_{0}.
∎
C.3 Proof of Corollary 5.1
Proof of Corollary 5.1.
This corollary follows by simplifying the expressions in Theorem 5.1.
We begin by analyzing the alternative distribution.
When m=1m=1, the general density formula simplifies to:
fY1Δ1​(y1)=11−Δ1​∑σ∈{−1,1}(BσΔ1​(y1)−AσΔ1​(y1))∨0.f_{Y_{1}}^{\Delta_{1}}(y_{1})=\frac{1}{1-\Delta_{1}}\sum_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}}\left(B_{\sigma}^{\Delta_{1}}(y_{1})-A_{\sigma}^{\Delta_{1}}(y_{1})\right)\vee 0.
Case σ=+1\sigma=+1.
Using the definitions from the theorem, we compute:
A+1Δ1​(y1)\displaystyle A_{+1}^{\Delta_{1}}(y_{1})
=max⁡{−y1,Δ1​y11−Δ1}=Δ1​y11−Δ1,\displaystyle=\max\left\{-y_{1},\frac{\Delta_{1}y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}}\right\}=\frac{\Delta_{1}y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}},
B+1Δ1​(y1)\displaystyle B_{+1}^{\Delta_{1}}(y_{1})
=min⁡{1−y1,1}=1−y1.\displaystyle=\min\left\{1-y_{1},1\right\}=1-y_{1}.
The corresponding contribution is:
(1−y1−Δ1​y11−Δ1)∨0=(1−y11−Δ1)∨0.\left(1-y_{1}-\frac{\Delta_{1}y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}}\right)\vee 0=\left(1-\frac{y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}}\right)\vee 0.
Case σ=−1\sigma=-1.
We have:
A−1Δ1​(y1)\displaystyle A_{-1}^{\Delta_{1}}(y_{1})
=max⁡{y1,0}=y1,\displaystyle=\max\left\{y_{1},0\right\}=y_{1},
B−1Δ1​(y1)\displaystyle B_{-1}^{\Delta_{1}}(y_{1})
=min⁡{1+y1,1−Δ1​y11−Δ1}=1−Δ1​y11−Δ1.\displaystyle=\min\left\{1+y_{1},1-\frac{\Delta_{1}y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}}\right\}=1-\frac{\Delta_{1}y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}}.
The resulting contribution is:
(1−Δ1​y11−Δ1−y1)∨0=(1−y11−Δ1)∨0.\left(1-\frac{\Delta_{1}y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}}-y_{1}\right)\vee 0=\left(1-\frac{y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}}\right)\vee 0.
Since both sign cases yield the same value, we obtain the final density by summing and applying the normalization:
fY1Δ1​(y1)=21−Δ1​(1−y11−Δ1),f_{Y_{1}}^{\Delta_{1}}(y_{1})=\frac{2}{1-\Delta_{1}}\left(1-\frac{y_{1}}{1-\Delta_{1}}\right),
which expands to the triangular form in (21).
Next, we consider the null distribution. When m=1m=1, the formula from Theorem 5.1 becomes:
fY1(y1)=∫u:y1<max⁡(u,1−u)2𝟏​(y1<min⁡(u,1−u))du.f_{Y_{1}}(y_{1})=\int_{u:y_{1}<\max(u,1-u)}2^{{\bf 1}(y_{1}<\min(u,1-u))}\,{\mathrm{d}}u.
Case 0<y1≤1/20<y_{1}\leq 1/2.
In this case, y1<min⁡(u,1−u)y_{1}<\min(u,1-u) if and only if u∈(y1,1−y1)u\in(y_{1},1-y_{1}), and y1<max⁡(u,1−u)y_{1}<\max(u,1-u) for all u∈(0,1)u\in(0,1). Therefore, the density is:
fY1​(y1)=∫y11−y12​𝑑u+∫0y1𝑑u+∫1−y11𝑑u=2​(1−2​y1)+y1+y1=2​(1−y1).f_{Y_{1}}(y_{1})=\int_{y_{1}}^{1-y_{1}}2\,{\mathrm{d}}u+\int_{0}^{y_{1}}{\mathrm{d}}u+\int_{1-y_{1}}^{1}{\mathrm{d}}u=2(1-2y_{1})+y_{1}+y_{1}=2(1-y_{1}).
Case 1/2<y1<11/2<y_{1}<1.
Here, y1≥min⁡(u,1−u)y_{1}\geq\min(u,1-u), so the integrand is always 1. The condition y1<max⁡(u,1−u)y_{1}<\max(u,1-u) is satisfied when u∈(0,1−y1)∪(y1,1)u\in(0,1-y_{1})\cup(y_{1},1), yielding:
fY1​(y1)=∫01−y1𝑑u+∫y11𝑑u=(1−y1)+(1−y1)=2​(1−y1).f_{Y_{1}}(y_{1})=\int_{0}^{1-y_{1}}{\mathrm{d}}u+\int_{y_{1}}^{1}{\mathrm{d}}u=(1-y_{1})+(1-y_{1})=2(1-y_{1}).
In both cases, we conclude that fY1​(y1)=2​(1−y1)f_{Y_{1}}(y_{1})=2(1-y_{1}) for all y1∈(0,1)y_{1}\in(0,1), completing the proof.
∎
C.4 Proof of Lemma C.1
Proof of Lemma C.1.
The randomness in this setting arises from the pseudorandom variables UU and π\pi. Given the fixed minimal unit ℐζ{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}, we aim to compute
ℙ1(U≤r,π(wℓ)=wℓ′ for ℓ∈[m]|ℐζ).{\mathbb{P}}_{1}\bigl(U\leq r,\,\pi(w_{\ell})=w_{\ell}^{\prime}\text{ for }\ell\in[m]~|~{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}\bigr).
(100)
For each permutation π\pi of the vocabulary, we can evaluate the probability that U∈[0,r]U\in[0,r] under the constraint that π⁡(wℓ)=wℓ′\pi(w_{\ell})=w_{\ell}^{\prime} for all ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m]. Recall the definition of the inverse transform decoder: for any token ww,
𝒮inv(𝑷,ζ)=wif and only if∑w′∈𝒲Pw′⋅𝟏{π(w′)<π(w)}≤U≤∑w′∈𝒲Pw′⋅𝟏{π(w′)≤π(w)}.\mathcal{S}^{\text{inv}}(\bm{P},\zeta)=w\quad\text{if and only if}\quad\sum_{w^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}P_{w^{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{\pi(w^{\prime})<\pi(w)\}}\leq U\leq\sum_{w^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}P_{w^{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{\pi(w^{\prime})\leq\pi(w)\}}.
In our setting, knowing that π⁡(wℓ)=wℓ′\pi(w_{\ell})=w_{\ell}^{\prime} for all ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m] and using the definition that aπ,wℓ(t)=∑j=1wℓPt,π⁡(j)a_{\pi,\,w_{\ell}}^{(t)}=\sum_{j=1}^{w_{\ell}}P_{t,\pi(j)}, the above condition becomes, for each t∈ℐℓYt\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell} (where wℓw_{\ell} is the token associated with sub-block ℐℓY{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}),
aπ−1,wℓ′−1(t)\displaystyle a_{\pi^{-1},\,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}
=∑w′∈𝒲Pt,w′⋅𝟏{π(w′)<wℓ′}≤U≤∑w′∈𝒲Pt,w′⋅𝟏{π(w′)≤wℓ′}=aπ−1,wℓ′(t).\displaystyle=\sum_{w^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}P_{t,w^{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{\pi(w^{\prime})<w_{\ell}^{\prime}\}}\leq U\leq\sum_{w^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}P_{t,w^{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{\pi(w^{\prime})\leq w_{\ell}^{\prime}\}}=a_{\pi^{-1},\,w_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{(t)}.
(101)
The corresponding feasible region for UU is thus the intersection
⋂ℓ=1m⋂t∈ℐℓY(aπ−1,wℓ′−1(t),aπ−1,wℓ′(t)).\displaystyle\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{m}\bigcap_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\left(a_{\pi^{-1},w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)},\,a_{\pi^{-1},w_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{(t)}\right).
(102)
Summing over all permutations π\pi and all tuples of mutually distinct tokens w1′,…,wm′w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime} (that is, distinct\mathrm{distinct}), we obtain:
ℙ1(U≤r,π(wℓ)=wℓ′ for ℓ∈[m]|ℐζ)\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{1}\bigl(U\leq r,\,\pi(w_{\ell})=w_{\ell}^{\prime}\text{ for }\ell\in[m]~|~{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}\bigr)
(103)
=\displaystyle=\;
1|𝒲|!​∑π∈Perm⁡(𝒲)π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,ℓ∈[m]ℙ⁡(U∈⋂ℓ=1m⋂t∈ℐℓY(aπ,wℓ′−1(t),aπ,wℓ′(t))∩[0,r]).\displaystyle\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathrm{Perm}({{\mathcal{W}}})\\
\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\;\ell\in[m]\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(U\in\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{m}\bigcap_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\left(a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)},\,a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{(t)}\right)\cap[0,r]\right).
(104)
Note that since we sum over all permutations π\pi, the roles of π\pi and π−1\pi^{-1} are interchangeable in the expression above. Hence, we replace π\pi with π−1\pi^{-1} in the last equation for notational simplicity.
To obtain the normalization constant (that is, the denominator of the conditional probability), we set r=1r=1 and sum over all distinct w1′,…,wm′w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}:
∑w1′,…,wm′distinctℙ(U≤1,π(wℓ)=wℓ′ for ℓ∈[m]|ℐζ)\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(U\leq 1,\,\pi(w_{\ell})=w_{\ell}^{\prime}\text{ for }\ell\in[m]~|~{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}\bigr)
(105)
=\displaystyle=\;
1|𝒲|!​∑w1′,…,wm′distinct∑π∈Perm⁡(𝒲)π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,ℓ∈[m]ℙ⁡(U∈⋂ℓ=1m⋂t∈ℐℓY(aπ,wℓ′−1(t),aπ,wℓ′(t))∩[0,1]).\displaystyle\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathrm{Perm}({{\mathcal{W}}})\\
\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\;\ell\in[m]\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(U\in\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{m}\bigcap_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\left(a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)},\,a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{(t)}\right)\cap[0,1]\right).
(106)
Thus, the conditional probability can be expressed as
ℙ(U≤r,π(wℓ)=wℓ′ for ℓ∈[m]|ℐζ)\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(U\leq r,\,\pi(w_{\ell})=w_{\ell}^{\prime}\text{ for }\ell\in[m]\,\big|\,{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}\bigr)
(107)
=\displaystyle=\;
1|𝒲|!​∑π∈Perm⁡(𝒲)π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,ℓ∈[m]ℙ⁡(U∈⋂ℓ=1m⋂t∈ℐℓY(aπ,wℓ′−1(t),aπ,wℓ′(t))∩[0,r])1|𝒲|!​∑w1′,…,wm′distinct∑π∈Perm⁡(𝒲)π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,ℓ∈[m]ℙ⁡(U∈⋂ℓ=1m⋂t∈ℐℓY(aπ,wℓ′−1(t),aπ,wℓ′(t))).\displaystyle\frac{\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathrm{Perm}({{\mathcal{W}}})\\
\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\;\ell\in[m]\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(U\in\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{m}\bigcap_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\left(a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)},\,a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{(t)}\right)\cap[0,r]\right)}{\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathrm{Perm}({{\mathcal{W}}})\\
\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\;\ell\in[m]\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(U\in\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{m}\bigcap_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\left(a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)},\,a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{(t)}\right)\right)}.
(108)
∎
C.5 Proof of Theorem C.1
Proof of Theorem C.1.
In this proof, we aim to show that the absolute error
|𝔼1,𝐏ℐζ⁡[J⁡(U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))]−1Im​(𝚫¯)​∫[0,1]m(∫ℐ⁡(𝐱,𝚫¯)J⁡(u,𝐱)​du)​𝟏ℐ⁡(𝐱,𝚫¯)≠∅​𝑑𝐱|\displaystyle\left|\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}}}\left[J(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\ldots,\eta(\pi(w_{m})))\right]-\frac{1}{I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}})}\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\left(\int_{{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})}J(u,\bm{x})\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right){\bf 1}_{{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})\neq\emptyset}{\mathrm{d}}\bm{x}\right|
(109)
converges to zero as the vocabulary size |𝒲||{{\mathcal{W}}}| tends to infinity, provided that the underlying NTP distributions 𝑷ℐζ\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}} satisfy Assumption 5.1.
In the expression (109), we simplify the original target integral by letting 𝒙=(x1,…,xm)\bm{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}) and d𝒙=dx1⋯dxm{\mathrm{d}}\bm{x}={\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m}, and by rewriting the normalization constant via
ℐ⁡(𝒙,𝚫¯)=[maxℓ∈[m]⁡{Δ¯ℓ​xℓ},minℓ∈[m]⁡{1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓ​xℓ}],{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})=\left[\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}\{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}\},\;\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}\{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}\}\right],
(110)
where we define Δ¯ℓ:=maxt∈ℐℓY⁡Δt{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}:=\max_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\Delta_{t}.
As specified in Definition C.1, the NTP distributions 𝑷ℐζ\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}} are assumed to belong to the class 𝒬type⁡(𝑷ℐζ),𝚫{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathrm{type}(\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}}),\bm{\Delta}} from Assumption 5.1.
Let J:[0,1]m+1→[0,∞)J\colon[0,1]^{m+1}\to[0,\infty) be a 1-Lipschitz function.
Without loss of generality, we assume J⁡(0,0,…,0)=0J(0,0,\ldots,0)=0. This is justified because replacing JJ with J−CJ-C for any constant CC does not affect the absolute error term in (109) by using the fact that Im​(𝚫¯)=∫[0,1]m|ℐ⁡(𝒙,𝚫¯)|⋅𝟏ℐ⁡(𝒙,𝚫¯)≠∅​𝑑𝒙.I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}})=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}|{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})|\cdot{\bf 1}_{{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})\neq\emptyset}\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{x}.
We are now ready to analyze the asymptotic behavior of 𝔼1,𝐏ℐζ⁡[J⁡(U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))].\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}}}\left[J(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\ldots,\eta(\pi(w_{m})))\right].
An exact formulation is provided by Corollary C.1:
𝔼1,𝐏ℐζ⁡[J⁡(U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))]\displaystyle\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}}}\left[J(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\ldots,\eta(\pi(w_{m})))\right]
=∑w1′,…,wm′distinct∑π∈Perm⁡(𝒲)π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ∈[m]∫maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)J⁡(u,η⁡(w1′),…,η⁡(wm′))​𝑑u​𝟏minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)≥maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)∑w1′,…,wm′distinct∑π∈Perm⁡(𝒲)π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ∈[m]ℙ⁡(U∈⋂ℓ=1m⋂t∈ℐℓY(aπ,wℓ′−1(t),aπ,wℓ′(t))),\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathrm{Perm}({{\mathcal{W}}})\\
\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\in[m]\end{subarray}}\int_{\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}}^{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}}J(u,\eta(w^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\eta(w^{\prime}_{m}))\,{\mathrm{d}}u{\bf 1}_{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}\geq\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}}}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathrm{Perm}({{\mathcal{W}}})\\
\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\in[m]\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(U\in\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{m}\bigcap_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\left(a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)},a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}\right)\right)},
(111)
where minℓ,t\min\limits_{\ell,t} denotes minℓ⁡mint∈ℐℓY\min_{\ell}\min_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}} and similarly maxℓ,t\max\limits_{\ell,t} denotes maxℓ⁡maxt∈ℐℓY\max_{\ell}\max_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}} for simplicity.
Numerator.
We begin by analyzing the numerator of (111):
1|𝒲|!​∑w1′,…,wm′distinct∑π∈Perm⁡(𝒲)π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ∈[m](∫maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)J⁡(u,η⁡(w1′),…,η⁡(wm′))​𝑑u)​𝟏minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)≥maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)\displaystyle\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathrm{Perm}({{\mathcal{W}}})\\
\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\in[m]\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}}^{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}}J(u,\eta(w^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\eta(w^{\prime}_{m}))\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right){\bf 1}_{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}\geq\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}}
(112)
Our first step is to rewrite this expression by introducing a random permutation π\pi. The sum over permutations can then be expressed as an expectation:
𝔼π⁡[∑w1′,…,wm′distinct𝟏π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ∈[m]​∫maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)J⁡(u,η⁡(w1′),…,η⁡(wm′))​du⋅𝟏minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)≥maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)].\displaystyle\EB_{\pi}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}\\
\text{distinct}\end{subarray}}{\bf 1}_{\pi(w^{\prime}_{\ell})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\in[m]}\int_{\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}}^{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}}J(u,\eta(w^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\eta(w^{\prime}_{m}))\,{\mathrm{d}}u\cdot{\bf 1}_{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}\geq\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}}\right].
By linearity of expectation, we can exchange the expectation and the outer summation over the source tokens w1′,…,wm′w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}:
∑w1′,…,wm′distinct𝔼π⁡[𝟏π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ∈[m]​∫maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)J⁡(u,η⁡(w1′),…,η⁡(wm′))​du⋅𝟏minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)≥maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)].\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}\\
\text{distinct}\end{subarray}}\EB_{\pi}\left[{\bf 1}_{\pi(w^{\prime}_{\ell})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\in[m]}\int_{\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}}^{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}}J(u,\eta(w^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\eta(w^{\prime}_{m}))\,{\mathrm{d}}u\cdot{\bf 1}_{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}\geq\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}}\right].
(113)
For any fixed set of distinct source tokens {wℓ′}ℓ=1m\{w^{\prime}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m} and distinct target tokens {wℓ}ℓ=1m\{w_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m}, let
C={π(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ∈[m]}C=\{\pi(w^{\prime}_{\ell})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\in[m]\}
denote the event that π\pi maps wℓ′w^{\prime}_{\ell} to wℓw_{\ell} for each ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m]. This event corresponds to a specific set of permutations, and its total count is (|𝒲|−m)!(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-m)!. By the law of total expectation, we can write
𝔼⁡[X⋅𝟏C]=𝔼⁡[X∣C]⋅ℙ⁡(C),whereℙ⁡(C)=(|𝒲|−m)!|𝒲|!\EB[X\cdot{\bf 1}_{C}]=\EB[X\mid C]\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(C),\quad\text{where}\quad{\mathbb{P}}(C)=\frac{(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-m)!}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|!}
for any random variable XX.
Substituting this into (113), we obtain the following simplified form:
1∏ℓ=0m−1(|𝒲|−ℓ)∑w1′,…,wm′distinct𝔼π[(∫maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)​Δtminℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)J(u,η(w′1),…,η(w′m))du)⋅𝟏minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)≥maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)|∀ℓ,π(w′ℓ)=wℓ].\begin{split}\frac{1}{\prod_{\ell=0}^{m-1}(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-\ell)}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\EB_{\pi}\Bigg[\left(\int_{\max\limits_{\ell,t}a^{(t)}_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}\Delta_{t}}^{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}}J\left(u,\eta(w^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\eta(w^{\prime}_{m})\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot{\bf 1}_{\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)}\geq\max\limits_{\ell,t}a^{(t)}_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}}\,\Bigg|\,\forall\ell,\ \pi(w^{\prime}_{\ell})=w_{\ell}\Bigg].\end{split}
(114)
Next, we simplify the integration limits, maxℓ,t⁡aπ,w′​ℓ−1(t)\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}\ell-1}^{(t)} and minℓ,t⁡aπ,w′​ℓ(t)\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}\ell}^{(t)}, by applying concentration inequalities under the conditional distribution π|C\pi\mid C.
In particular, applying Lemma C.8 to the maximum function, we obtain
|maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)−maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡(wℓ′−1)​Δt|𝒲|−1|≤maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡|aπ,wℓ′−1(t)−(wℓ′−1)​Δt|𝒲|−1|.\displaystyle\left|\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}-\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\frac{(w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left|a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}-\frac{(w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|.
(115)
Using Lemma C.3 below, we bound the right-hand side of (115) by O⁡(1|𝒲|+ε|𝒲|​log⁡|𝒲|)O\left(\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+\sqrt{{\varepsilon}_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\right), which vanishes as |𝒲|→∞|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty. The proof of Lemma C.3 can be found in Section C.6.
Lemma C.3 (Concentration of maxℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′−1(t)\max\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}).
Under Assumption 5.1, let π\pi be a uniformly random permutation over 𝒲{{\mathcal{W}}}. Then, for any distinct source tokens {wℓ′}ℓ=1m\{w^{\prime}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m} and target tokens {wℓ}ℓ=1m\{w_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m}, we have
maxw1′,…,wm′distinct\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}
𝔼π[maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY|aπ,wℓ′−1(t)−(wℓ′−1)​Δt|𝒲|−1||∀ℓ∈[m],π(wℓ′)=wℓ]\displaystyle\EB_{\pi}\left[\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left|a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}-\frac{(w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|\Bigg|\forall\ell\in[m],\pi(w^{\prime}_{\ell})=w_{\ell}\right]
≤O⁡(m)⋅maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡(1|𝒲|+Pt,(2)​log⁡|𝒲|+Pt,(2)​log⁡|𝒲|)\displaystyle\leq O(m)\cdot\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+\sqrt{P_{t,(2)}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+P_{t,(2)}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\right)
(116)
where Δt\Delta_{t} is the regularity level for 𝐏t\bm{P}_{t}, and O⁡(⋅)O(\cdot) hides universal constants.
Note that
aπ,wℓ′(t)=∑j=1wℓ′Pt,π⁡(j)=1−Δt+∑j=1wℓ′−1Pt,π⁡(j)=1−Δt+aπ,wℓ′−1(t).a_{\pi,\,w_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{(t)}=\sum_{j=1}^{w_{\ell}^{\prime}}P_{t,\pi(j)}=1-\Delta_{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}P_{t,\pi(j)}=1-\Delta_{t}+a_{\pi,\,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}.
Using this identity, we can approximate the lower integration limit minℓ,t⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)\min\limits_{\ell,t}a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}}^{(t)} as follows:
|minℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡aπ,wℓ′(t)−minℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡[1−(|𝒲|−wℓ′)​Δt|𝒲|−1]|\displaystyle\left|\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\

≤maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡|aπ,wℓ′(t)−(1−(|𝒲|−wℓ′)​Δt|𝒲|−1)|\displaystyle\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\

t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left|a_{\pi,w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}^{(t)}-\frac{(w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|.
(117)
By using this approximation to the upper and lower integral limits, we assert that quantity (114) will be close to the following quantity:
1∏ℓ=0m−1(|𝒲|−ℓ)∑w1′,…,wm′distinct𝔼π[(∫maxℓ,t⁡wℓ′−1|𝒲|−1​Δtminℓ,t⁡[1−|𝒲|−wℓ′|𝒲|−1​Δt]J(u,η(w′1),…,η(w′m))du)⋅𝟏minℓ,t⁡[1−(|𝒲|−wℓ′)​Δt|𝒲|−1]≥maxℓ,t⁡(wℓ′−1)​Δt|𝒲|−1|∀ℓ,π(w′ℓ)=wℓ]\begin{split}\frac{1}{\prod_{\ell=0}^{m-1}(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-\ell)}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\EB_{\pi}\Bigg[\left(\int_{\max\limits_{\ell,t}\frac{w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\Delta_{t}}^{\min\limits_{\ell,t}\left[1-\frac{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-w^{\prime}_{\ell}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\Delta_{t}\right]}J\left(u,\eta(w^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\eta(w^{\prime}_{m})\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot{\bf 1}_{\min\limits_{\ell,t}\left[1-\frac{(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-w^{\prime}_{\ell})\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right]\geq\max\limits_{\ell,t}\frac{(w^{\prime}_{\ell}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}}\,\Bigg|\,\forall\ell,\ \pi(w^{\prime}_{\ell})=w_{\ell}\Bigg]\end{split}
(118)
This is because
|(114)−(118)|\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:numerator-simplified-1}-\eqref{eq:numerator-simplified-1.1}|
≤(a)4∥J∥∞⋅maxw1′,…,wm′distinct𝔼π[maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY|aπ,wℓ′−1(t)−(wℓ′−1)​Δt|𝒲|−1||π(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ]\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}4\|J\|_{\infty}\cdot\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\EB_{\pi}\left[\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left|a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}-\frac{(w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|\Bigg|\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\right]
≤(b)​O​(m)⋅‖J‖∞⋅maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡(1|𝒲|+Pt,(2)​log⁡|𝒲|+Pt,(2)​log⁡|𝒲|),\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}O(m)\cdot\|J\|_{\infty}\cdot\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+\sqrt{P_{t,(2)}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+P_{t,(2)}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\right),
(119)
where (a)(a) follows from Lemma C.10, with ‖J‖∞:=sup𝒙∈[0,1]m+1|J⁡(𝒙)|\|J\|_{\infty}:=\sup_{\bm{x}\in[0,1]^{m+1}}|J(\bm{x})| denoting the supremum norm of JJ over [0,1]m+1[0,1]^{m+1} and (b)(b) follows from Lemma C.3, where O⁡(1)O(1) denotes a universal constant.
Therefore, it suffices to analyze the expression in (118).
Once the upper and lower integration limits are approximated, the entire integrand becomes independent of π\pi, allowing us to safely remove the expectation over π\pi.
To study the resulting deterministic quantity, we define the function
Φ⁡(x1,…,xm)\displaystyle\Phi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
=(∫maxℓ,t⁡Δt​xℓminℓ,t⁡[1−Δt+Δt​xℓ]J(u,x1,…,xm)du)𝟏{minℓ,t{1−Δt+Δtxℓ}≥maxℓ,tΔtxℓ}\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\max\limits_{\ell,t}\Delta_{t}x_{\ell}}^{\min\limits_{\ell,t}[1-\Delta_{t}+\Delta_{t}x_{\ell}]}J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}){\mathrm{d}}u\right){\bf 1}_{\left\{\min\limits_{\ell,t}\{1-\Delta_{t}+\Delta_{t}x_{\ell}\}\geq\max\limits_{\ell,t}\Delta_{t}x_{\ell}\right\}}
=(∫maxℓ∈[m]⁡Δ¯ℓ​xℓminℓ∈[m]⁡[1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓ​xℓ]J(u,x1,…,xm)du)𝟏{minℓ∈[m][1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓxℓ]≥maxℓ∈[m]Δ¯ℓxℓ},\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}}^{\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}[1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}]}J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}){\mathrm{d}}u\right){\bf 1}_{\left\{\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}[1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}]\geq\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}\right\}},
where Δ¯ℓ:=maxt∈ℐℓY⁡Δt{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}:=\max_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\Delta_{t} denotes the maximum regularity level associated with sub-block ℐℓY{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}.
It is straightforward to verify that Φ\Phi is Lipschitz continuous with respect to the L∞L^{\infty} norm on [0,1]m[0,1]^{m}, owing to the Lipschitz continuity of JJ and the boundedness of the variables {Δ¯ℓ,xℓ}ℓ=1m⊆[0,1]\{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell},x_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m}\subseteq[0,1].
With this definition, we can rewrite (118) as
(118)
=1∏i=0m−1(|𝒲|−i)​∑w1′,…,wm′distinctΦ⁡(η⁡(w1′),…,η⁡(wm′))\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-i)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\Phi(\eta(w_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\eta(w_{m}^{\prime}))
=(a)​1|𝒲|m​∑w1′,…,wm′Φ⁡(η⁡(w1′),…,η⁡(wm′))+O⁡(‖J‖∞|𝒲|),\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|^{m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\Phi(\eta(w_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\eta(w_{m}^{\prime}))+O\left(\frac{\|J\|_{\infty}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\right),
=(b)∫[0,1]mΦ(x1,…,xm)dx1⋯dxm+O(1|𝒲|)+O(‖J‖∞|𝒲|),\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\Phi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}){\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m}+O\left(\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\right)+O\left(\frac{\|J\|_{\infty}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\right),
(120)
where
(a)
follows from the expansion ∏i=0m−1(|𝒲|−i)=|𝒲|m​[1+O⁡(m2|𝒲|)]\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-i)=|{{\mathcal{W}}}|^{m}\left[1+O\left(\frac{m^{2}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\right)\right] and the observation that the number of non-fully-distinct mm-tuples (η⁡(w1′),…,η⁡(wm′))(\eta(w_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\eta(w_{m}^{\prime})) is at most O⁡(m2​|𝒲|m−1)O(m^{2}|{{\mathcal{W}}}|^{m-1}), with each summand bounded in magnitude by ‖J‖∞\|J\|_{\infty};
(b)
follows from approximating the Riemann sum over the uniform grid {η⁡(w′)=(w′−1)/(|𝒲|−1):w′∈𝒲}\{\eta(w^{\prime})=(w^{\prime}-1)/(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1):w^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{W}}}\} of mesh size 1/(|𝒲|−1)1/(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1), which discretizes [0,1][0,1] evenly. Since Φ\Phi is Lipschitz, the resulting Riemann sum converges to the Lebesgue integral with error O⁡(1/|𝒲|)O(1/|{{\mathcal{W}}}|) per coordinate, yielding a total approximation error of O⁡(1/|𝒲|)O(1/|{{\mathcal{W}}}|).
Combining the results above, we conclude that
Numerator of(111)=(114)=(119)(118)+o(1)=(120)∫[0,1]mΦ(x1,…,xm)dx1⋯dxm+o(1),\displaystyle\text{Numerator of}~\eqref{eq:target-expectation-0}=\eqref{eq:numerator-simplified-1}\overset{\eqref{eq:integral-approximation-error1}}{=}\eqref{eq:numerator-simplified-1.1}+o(1)\overset{\eqref{eq:integral-approximation-error2}}{=}\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\Phi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m}+o(1),
=∫[0,1]m∫maxℓ∈[m]⁡Δ¯ℓ​xℓminℓ∈[m]⁡[1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓ​xℓ]J(u,x1,…,xm)𝟏{minℓ∈[m][1−Δ¯ℓ+Δ¯ℓxℓ]≥maxℓ∈[m]Δ¯ℓxℓ}dudx1⋯dxm+o(1)\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\int_{\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}}^{\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}[1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}]}J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}){\bf 1}_{\left\{\min\limits_{\ell\in[m]}[1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}]\geq\max\limits_{\ell\in[m]}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}x_{\ell}\right\}}{\mathrm{d}}u{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m}+o(1)
=∫[0,1]m(∫ℐ⁡(𝒙,𝚫¯)J⁡(u,𝒙)​𝑑u)​𝟏ℐ⁡(𝒙,𝚫¯)≠∅​𝑑𝒙+o⁡(1),\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\left(\int_{{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})}J(u,\bm{x})\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right){\bf 1}_{{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})\neq\emptyset}{\mathrm{d}}\bm{x}+o(1),
(121)
where the o⁡(1)o(1) term vanishes uniformly as |𝒲|→∞|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty, over all 1-Lipschitz functions JJ, all 𝚫¯∈[Δ,1−δ]m\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}\in[\Delta,1-\delta]^{m}, and all 𝑷ℐζ∈𝒬τ,𝚫\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}}.
Denominator.
We now turn to the denominator of (111). Since it corresponds to the numerator with the constant function J≡1J\equiv 1, we set J⁡(u,x1,…,xm):=1J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}):=1 and obtain
Denominator of (111)
=1|𝒲|!​∑w1′,…,wm′distinct∑π∈Perm⁡(𝒲)π⁡(wℓ′)=wℓ​∀ℓ∈[m]ℙ⁡(U∈⋂ℓ∈[m]⋂t∈ℐℓY(aπ,wℓ′−1(t),aπ,wℓ′(t)))\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\\
