=∫[0,1]m|ℐ⁡(𝒙,𝚫¯)|⋅𝟏ℐ⁡(𝒙,𝚫¯)≠∅​𝑑𝒙+o⁡(1)\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}|{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})|\cdot{\bf 1}_{{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{x},\bar{\bm{\Delta}})\neq\emptyset}\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{x}+o(1)
=Im​(𝚫¯)+o​(1),\displaystyle=I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}})+o(1),
(122)
uniformly over all 1-Lipschitz functions JJ, all parameter vectors 𝚫¯∈[Δ,1−δ]m\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}\in[\Delta,1-\delta]^{m}, and all distributions 𝑷ℐζ\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta}} in the class 𝒬τ,𝚫{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}} defined in (86).
Finally, combining (121) and (122) yields the desired result.
∎
C.6 Proof of Lemma C.3
Proof of Lemma C.3.
The result follows from the concentration inequality in Lemma C.7, which applies to sums over randomly permuted arrays.
Let
C={π(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ∈[m]}C=\{\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\in[m]\}
denote the event that the random permutation π\pi maps each wℓ′w_{\ell}^{\prime} to wℓw_{\ell}.
To apply Lemma C.7, we fix an index ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m] and define
bi,j(t)=Pt,j⋅𝟏{i≤wℓ′,j∉{w1,…,wm}},for ​i,j∈𝒲.b^{(t)}_{i,j}=P_{t,j}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{i\leq w_{\ell}^{\prime}\,,\;j\not\in\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{m}\}\}},\quad\text{for }i,j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}.
Recall that aπ,wℓ′−1(t)=∑j=1wℓ′−1Pt,π⁡(j)a_{\pi,\,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}=\sum_{j=1}^{w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}P_{t,\pi(j)} and a direct calculation shows that for any t∈ℐℓYt\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell},
|∑j∈𝒲bj,π⁡(j)(t)−aπ,wℓ′−1(t)|≤m​Pt,(2).\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}b^{(t)}_{j,\pi(j)}-a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}\right|\leq mP_{t,(2)}.
(123)
Note that, conditioned on the event CC, the permutation π\pi is uniformly distributed as a bijection from 𝒲∖{w1′,…,wm′}{{\mathcal{W}}}\setminus\{w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\} to 𝒲∖{w1,…,wm}{{\mathcal{W}}}\setminus\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{m}\}. Therefore,
𝔼π⁡[∑j∈𝒲bj,π⁡(j)(t)|C]\displaystyle\EB_{\pi}\left[\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}b^{(t)}_{j,\pi(j)}\bigg|C\right]
=∑j∈𝒲∖{w1′,…,wm′}𝔼π⁡[Pt,π⁡(j)​𝟏j≤wℓ′|C]\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}\setminus\{w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\}}\EB_{\pi}\left[P_{t,\pi(j)}{\bf 1}_{j\leq w_{\ell}^{\prime}}\,\middle|\,C\right]
=|{j≤wℓ′:j∉{w1′,…,wm′}}|⋅1|𝒲|−m⋅∑j∈𝒲∖{w1,…,wm}Pt,j.\displaystyle=\left|\left\{j\leq w_{\ell}^{\prime}:j\notin\{w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\}\right\}\right|\cdot\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-m}\cdot\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}\setminus\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{m}\}}P_{t,j}.
Observe that
wℓ′−m≤|{j≤wℓ′:j∉{w1′,…,wm′}}|≤wℓ′−1.w_{\ell}^{\prime}-m\leq\left|\left\{j\leq w_{\ell}^{\prime}:j\notin\{w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\}\right\}\right|\leq w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1.
Using this, we obtain
||{j≤wℓ′:j∉{w1′,…,wm′}}||𝒲|−m−wℓ′−1|𝒲|−1|\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left|\left\{j\leq w_{\ell}^{\prime}:j\notin\{w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\}\right\}\right|}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-m}-\frac{w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|
≤m−1|𝒲|−m+(wℓ′−1)​(m−1)(|𝒲|−m)​(|𝒲|−1)=O⁡(m|𝒲|).\displaystyle\leq\frac{m-1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-m}+\frac{(w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1)(m-1)}{(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-m)(|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1)}=O\!\left(\frac{m}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\right).
(124)
On the other hand, since ∑j∈𝒲∖{wt}Pt,j=Δt\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}\setminus\{w_{t}\}}P_{t,j}=\Delta_{t}, we also have
|∑j∈𝒲∖{w1,…,wm}Pt,j−∑j∈𝒲∖{wt}Pt,j|≤(m−1)​Pt,(2).\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}\setminus\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{m}\}}P_{t,j}-\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}\setminus\{w_{t}\}}P_{t,j}\right|\leq(m-1)P_{t,(2)}.
(125)
Putting the bounds together, we conclude that
|𝔼π⁡[∑j∈𝒲bj,π⁡(j)(t)|C]−(wℓ′−1)​Δt|𝒲|−1|≤2​m​(Pt,(2)+1|𝒲|),∀t∈ℐℓY.\displaystyle\left|\EB_{\pi}\left[\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}b^{(t)}_{j,\pi(j)}\,\middle|\,C\right]-\frac{(w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|\leq 2m\left(P_{t,(2)}+\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\right),\quad\forall t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}.
(126)
Thus, Lemma C.7 applies to ∑j∈𝒲bj,π⁡(j)(t)\sum_{j\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}b^{(t)}_{j,\pi(j)} for each t∈ℐℓYt\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell} and ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m].
More specifically, combining (123) and (126), for any λ>0\lambda>0, we have that with probability at least 1−λ1-\lambda,
|aπ,wℓ′−1(t)−(wℓ′−1)​Δt|𝒲|−1|≤O⁡(m)⋅(wℓ′|𝒲|​∑j=2|𝒲|Pt,(j)2​log⁡1λ+Pt,(2)​log⁡1λ+1|𝒲|),\displaystyle\left|a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}-\frac{(w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|\leq O(m)\cdot\left(\sqrt{\frac{w_{\ell}^{\prime}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\sum_{j=2}^{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}P_{t,(j)}^{2}\log\frac{1}{\lambda}}+P_{t,(2)}\log\frac{1}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\right),
(127)
where a universal constant hidden in O⁡(1)O(1).
Finally, we apply a union bound over all choices of distinct tokens w1′,…,wm′w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime} and all t∈ℐℓYt\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}, ℓ∈[m]\ell\in[m]. Setting λ=1m​|𝒲|m+1\lambda=\frac{1}{m\,|{{\mathcal{W}}}|^{m+1}}, we obtain
supw1′,…,wm′distinct𝔼π[maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY|aπ,wℓ′−1(t)−(wℓ′−1)​Δt|𝒲|−1||π(wℓ′)=wℓ,∀ℓ]\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{m}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{distinct}\end{subarray}}\EB_{\pi}\left[\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left|a_{\pi,w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}-\frac{(w_{\ell}^{\prime}-1)\Delta_{t}}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|-1}\right|\,\Bigg|\,\pi(w_{\ell}^{\prime})=w_{\ell},\forall\ell\right]
(128)
≤O⁡(m)⋅maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡(1|𝒲|+∑j=2|𝒲|Pt,(j)2​log⁡|𝒲|+Pt,(2)​log⁡|𝒲|)\displaystyle\leq O(m)\cdot\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+\sqrt{\sum_{j=2}^{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}P_{t,(j)}^{2}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+P_{t,(2)}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\right)
(129)
≤O⁡(m)⋅maxℓ∈[m]t∈ℐℓY⁡(1|𝒲|+Pt,(2)​log⁡|𝒲|+Pt,(2)​log⁡|𝒲|).\displaystyle\leq O(m)\cdot\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in[m]\\
t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+\sqrt{P_{t,(2)}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}+P_{t,(2)}\log|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\right).
(130)
∎
C.7 Proof of Lemma 7.7
Proof of Lemma 7.7.
As we focus on a single block, the sub-block index kk is omitted, following the convention in the proof of Theorem 5.1.
For simplicity, we also write Δ\Delta instead of Δ𝒱\Delta_{{\mathcal{V}}}.
For a minimal unit 𝒱=ℐζ{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{I}}^{\zeta} containing mm sub-blocks, we represent its associated pivotal statistics Y𝒱Y_{{\mathcal{V}}} as the vector 𝒀=(Y1,…,Ym)\bm{Y}=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}), where each component corresponds to a distinct sub-block. Since
Under Assumption 5.1, the set of NTP distributions in 𝒱{\mathcal{V}} can be rewritten using the notation 𝒬τ,𝚫{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}} from (86) as
{𝑷𝒱:𝑷𝒱⊆𝒫¯Δ}=⋃Δ≤𝚫≤1−δ𝒬𝒱,𝚫.\{\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}:\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\subseteq{\overline{{\mathcal{P}}}_{\Delta}}\}=\bigcup_{\Delta\leq\bm{\Delta}\leq 1-\delta}{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{V}},\bm{\Delta}}.
(131)
We aim to show that
lim sup|𝒲|→∞[𝔼0⁡[h⁡(𝐘)]+sup𝐏𝒱⊆𝒫¯Δlog⁡𝔼1,𝐏𝒱​[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))]]=supΔ≤𝚫¯L′​(h,𝚫¯),\limsup_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty}\left[\EB_{0}[h(\bm{Y})]+\sup_{\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\subseteq{\overline{{\mathcal{P}}}_{\Delta}}}\log\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}}[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))]\right]=\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}L^{\prime}(h,\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}),
(132)
where 𝚫¯=(Δ¯1,…,Δ¯mk)\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}=({\bar{\Delta}}_{1},\ldots,{\bar{\Delta}}_{m_{k}}), with each Δ¯ℓ{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell} defined in (17), and L′L^{\prime} is
defined by (given in (23))
L′​(h,𝚫¯)=𝔼f0⁡[h⁡(𝐘)]+log⁡𝔼f𝚫¯​[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))],L^{\prime}(h,\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}})=\EB_{f_{0}}[h(\bm{Y})]+\log\EB_{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}}[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))],
(23)
where f0f_{0} and f𝚫¯f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}} denote the asymptotic PDFs of 𝒀\bm{Y} under the null and alternative, given in Theorem 5.1 respectively.
To prove (132), it follows that
lim sup|𝒲|→∞[𝔼0⁡[h⁡(𝐘)]+sup𝐏𝒱⊆𝒫¯Δlog⁡𝔼1,𝐏𝒱​[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))]]\displaystyle\limsup_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty}\left[\EB_{0}[h(\bm{Y})]+\sup_{\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\subseteq{\overline{{\mathcal{P}}}_{\Delta}}}\log\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}}[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))]\right]
=(a)​lim sup|𝒲|→∞supΔ≤𝚫sup𝑷𝒱∈𝒬τ,𝚫[𝔼0⁡[h⁡(𝐘)]+log⁡𝔼1,𝐏𝒱​[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))]]\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\limsup_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty}\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{\Delta}}\sup_{\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}}}\left[\EB_{0}[h(\bm{Y})]+\log\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}}[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))]\right]
=(b)​supΔ≤𝚫≤1−δsup𝑷𝒱∈𝒬τ,𝚫lim sup|𝒲|→∞[𝔼0⁡[h⁡(𝐘)]+log⁡𝔼1,𝐏𝒱​[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))]]\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{\Delta}\leq 1-\delta}\sup_{\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}}}\limsup_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty}\left[\EB_{0}[h(\bm{Y})]+\log\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}}[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))]\right]
=(c)​supΔ≤𝚫≤1−δsup𝑷𝒱∈𝒬τ,𝚫[𝔼f0⁡[h⁡(𝐘)]+log⁡𝔼f𝚫¯​[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))]]\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{\Delta}\leq 1-\delta}\sup_{\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}}}\left[\EB_{f_{0}}[h(\bm{Y})]+\log\EB_{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}}[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))]\right]
(133)
=(d)​supΔ≤𝚫¯≤1−δ[𝔼f0⁡[h⁡(𝐘)]+log⁡𝔼f𝚫¯​[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))]]\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{=}\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}\leq 1-\delta}\left[\EB_{f_{0}}[h(\bm{Y})]+\log\EB_{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}}[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))]\right]
=supΔ≤𝚫¯≤1−δL′​(h,𝚫¯),\displaystyle=\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}\leq 1-\delta}L^{\prime}(h,\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}),
(134)
where (a) uses the equivalence in (131), (b) follows by exchanging the order of the lim sup\limsup and the suprema, which we will justify later, (c) follows from the weak convergence in Theorem C.1, and (d) simplifies the expression by eliminating the dependence on a single 𝑷𝒱\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}} and replacing 𝚫=(Δt)t∈𝒱\bm{\Delta}=(\Delta_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{V}}} with 𝚫¯=(Δ¯ℓ)ℓ∈[mk]\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}=({\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell})_{\ell\in[m_{k}]}, where Δ¯ℓ:=maxt∈ℐℓY⁡Δt{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}:=\max_{t\in{\mathcal{I}}^{Y}_{\ell}}\Delta_{t} for each ℓ\ell.
At this point, the proof is complete.
In the remainder, we establish the validity of the order exchange in step (b) above.
To this end, let us introduce a test function J:[0,1]m+1→ℝJ:[0,1]^{m+1}\to\mathbb{R} defined by
J⁡(u,x1,…,xm)=exp⁡(−h⁡(|u−x1|,…,|u−xm|)).\displaystyle J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})=\exp\big(-h(|u-x_{1}|,\ldots,|u-x_{m}|)\big).
Since hh is Lipschitz continuous and both the exponential and absolute value functions are locally Lipschitz on a bounded domain, their composition JJ is also Lipschitz continuous.
Theorem C.1 ensures that the convergence of the expectation of such a function is uniform. Specifically, it guarantees that
lim|𝒲|→∞supΔ≤𝚫≤1−δsup𝑷𝒱∈𝒬τ,𝚫|𝔼1,𝐏𝒱⁡[J⁡(U,η⁡(π⁡(w1)),…,η⁡(π⁡(wm)))]−ℒ𝚫¯​(J)|=0,\displaystyle\lim_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty}\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{\Delta}\leq 1-\delta}\sup_{\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}}}\left|\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\big[J(U,\eta(\pi(w_{1})),\ldots,\eta(\pi(w_{m})))\big]-\mathcal{L}_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(J)\right|=0,
(135)
where ℒ𝚫¯​(J)\mathcal{L}_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(J) is the asymptotic integral form
ℒ𝚫¯(J):=∫[0,1]m∫maxℓ⁡{Δℓ​xℓ}minℓ⁡{1−Δℓ+Δℓ​xℓ}J⁡(u,x1,…,xm)Im​(𝚫¯)du 1{mini{1−Δi+Δixi}≥maxi{Δixi}}dx1⋯dxm,\mathcal{L}_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(J):=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\int_{\max_{\ell}\{\Delta_{\ell}x_{\ell}\}}^{\min_{\ell}\{1-\Delta_{\ell}+\Delta_{\ell}x_{\ell}\}}\frac{J(u,x_{1},\dots,x_{m})}{I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}})}\,{\mathrm{d}}u\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\min_{i}\{1-\Delta_{i}+\Delta_{i}x_{i}\}\geq\max_{i}\{\Delta_{i}x_{i}\}\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m},
and 𝚫¯\bar{\bm{\Delta}} denotes the sub-block-level vector derived from 𝚫\bm{\Delta}.
By the definition of JJ, the uniform convergence in (135) is equivalent to the uniform convergence of the moment-generating function term:
lim|𝒲|→∞supΔ≤𝚫≤1−δsup𝑷𝒱∈𝒬τ,𝚫|𝔼1,𝐏𝒱⁡[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))]−ℒ𝚫¯​(exp⁡(−h))|=0,\displaystyle\lim_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|\to\infty}\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{\Delta}\leq 1-\delta}\sup_{\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tau,\bm{\Delta}}}\left|\EB_{1,\bm{P}_{{\mathcal{V}}}}\big[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))\big]-\mathcal{L}_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\exp(-h))\right|=0,
which is precisely (135).
Since ℒ𝚫¯​(exp⁡(−h))=𝔼f𝚫¯⁡[exp⁡(−h⁡(𝐘))]\mathcal{L}_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}}(\exp(-h))=\EB_{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}}[\exp(-h(\bm{Y}))] (by the equivalence in Corollary C.2), and the supremum is a nonexpansive operator (see Lemma C.8), we prove step (b).
∎
C.8 Proof of Lemma 7.8
Proof of Lemma 7.8.
Since we focus on a single block 𝒱{\mathcal{V}}, we write Δ\Delta instead of Δ𝒱\Delta_{{\mathcal{V}}} for simplicity. In this lemma, the alternative PDF is evaluated at the homogeneous regularity-level vector (Δ,…,Δ)(\Delta,\ldots,\Delta) and therefore depends only on the single parameter Δ\Delta.
For simplicity, we write
f1,Δ:=f(Δ,…,Δ),f_{1,\Delta}:=f_{(\Delta,\ldots,\Delta)},
to emphasize its dependence on the single parameter Δ\Delta.
For brevity, we define the truncated log-likelihood ratio as
hopt,M​(𝒚):=[log⁡f1,Δ​(𝒚)f0​(𝒚)][−M,M],\displaystyle h_{\mathrm{opt},M}(\bm{y}):=\Biggl[\log\frac{f_{1,\Delta}(\bm{y})}{f_{0}(\bm{y})}\Biggr]_{[-M,M]},
(136)
where [⋅][−M,M][\,\cdot\,]_{[-M,M]} denotes truncation to the interval [−M,M][-M,M].
Lemma C.4 (Properties of the alternative PDF).
Let f𝚫¯f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}} denote the joint alternative PDF of the pivotal statistic
vector 𝐘=(Y1,…,Ym)\bm{Y}=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}) under H1H_{1}, where the regularity levels are
𝚫¯=(Δ¯1,…,Δ¯m)\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}=({\bar{\Delta}}_{1},\ldots,{\bar{\Delta}}_{m}).
Its explicit form is given in Theorem 5.1.
Then, under Assumption 5.1,
•
f𝚫¯​(𝒚)f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y}) is strictly positive for 𝒚∈∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ¯ℓ)\bm{y}\in\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}), and equals 00 on the complement set [0,1]m∖∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ¯ℓ)[0,1]^{m}\setminus\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}).
•
The mapping (𝒚,𝚫¯)↦f𝚫¯​(𝒚)(\bm{y},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}})\mapsto f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})
is Lipschitz continuous on its domain
[0,1]m×[0,1−δ]m[0,1]^{m}\times[0,1-\delta]^{m}.
That is, there exists a universal constant C>0C>0 such that for any
(𝒚,𝚫¯),(𝒚′,𝚫¯′)∈[0,1]m×[0,1−δ]m(\bm{y},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}),(\bm{y}^{\prime},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime})\in[0,1]^{m}\times[0,1-\delta]^{m},
|f𝚫¯​(𝒚)−f𝚫¯′​(𝒚′)|≤C⋅(‖𝒚−𝒚′‖∞+‖𝚫¯−𝚫¯′‖∞).\big|f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})-f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y}^{\prime})\big|\;\leq\;C\cdot\Big(\|\bm{y}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+\|\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}-\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\Big).
Before proceeding with the proof, we first establish some properties of the PDF f1,Δf_{1,\Delta}. The proof of this lemma is provided in Section C.9.
With L′L^{\prime} defined in (23), we can decompose
L′​(hopt,M,𝚫¯′)\displaystyle L^{\prime}(h_{\text{opt},M},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime})
=𝔼f0⁡[hopt,M​(𝐘)]⏟(I)+log⁡𝔼f𝚫¯′​[exp⁡(−hopt,M​(𝐘))]⏟(II).\displaystyle=\underset{\text{(I)}}{\underbrace{\EB_{f_{0}}[h_{\text{opt},M}(\bm{Y})]}}+\underset{\text{(II)}}{\underbrace{\log\EB_{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime}}}[\exp(-h_{\text{opt},M}(\bm{Y}))]}}.
(137)
We then bound terms (I) and (II) separately.
Analysis of Term (I).
By Lemma C.4, f1,Δf_{1,\Delta} is supported on [0,1−Δ)m[0,1-\Delta)^{m} and is Lipschitz continuous in (𝒚,Δ)(\bm{y},\Delta). Since f0f_{0} coincides with f1,Δf_{1,\Delta} when Δ=0\Delta=0 (see Corollary C.2 and Lemma C.2), the null density f0​(𝒚)f_{0}(\bm{y}) is continuous and strictly positive on [0,1)m[0,1)^{m}. This ensures the existence of a finite upper bound for the following likelihood ratio:
M′:=supΔ′∈[0,1]sup𝒚∈[0,1]mf1,Δ′​(𝒚)inf𝒚∈[0,1−Δ)mf0(𝒚)<∞.M^{\prime}:=\sup_{\Delta^{\prime}\in[0,1]}\frac{\sup_{\bm{y}\in[0,1]^{m}}f_{1,\Delta^{\prime}}(\bm{y})}{\inf_{\bm{y}\in[0,1-\Delta)^{m}}f_{0}(\bm{y})}<\infty.
Importantly, M′M^{\prime} is independent of the clipping threshold MM. When M≥|log⁡M′|M\geq|\log M^{\prime}|, the log-likelihood ratio log⁡f1,Δ​(𝒀)f0​(𝒀)\log\tfrac{f_{1,\Delta}(\bm{Y})}{f_{0}(\bm{Y})} never exceeds MM, so the score function hopt,Mh_{\text{opt},M} can only be clipped at −M-M, which occurs when 𝒀\bm{Y} lies outside the support of f𝚫¯f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}, that is, outside [0,1−Δ)m[0,1-\Delta)^{m}.
𝔼f0⁡[hopt,M​(𝐘)]\displaystyle\EB_{f_{0}}[h_{\text{opt},M}(\bm{Y})]
=𝔼f0⁡[log⁡f1,Δ​(𝐘)f0​(𝐘)][−M,M]\displaystyle=\EB_{f_{0}}\left[\log\frac{f_{1,\Delta}(\bm{Y})}{f_{0}(\bm{Y})}\right]_{[-M,M]}
=∫[0,1−Δ)m[logf1,Δ​(𝒚)f0​(𝒚)][−M,M]f0(𝒚)d𝒚+∫[0,1]m∖[0,1−Δ)m(−M)f0(𝒚)d𝒚\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1-\Delta)^{m}}\left[\log\frac{f_{1,\Delta}(\bm{y})}{f_{0}(\bm{y})}\right]_{[-M,M]}f_{0}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y}+\int_{[0,1]^{m}\setminus[0,1-\Delta)^{m}}(-M)f_{0}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y}
≤∫[0,1−Δ)m(logM′)⋅f0(𝒚)d𝒚−M⋅ℙf0(𝒀∉[0,1−Δ)m)\displaystyle\leq\int_{[0,1-\Delta)^{m}}(\log M^{\prime})\cdot f_{0}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y}-M\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{0}}(\bm{Y}\notin[0,1-\Delta)^{m})
≤(logM′)⋅ℙf0(𝒀∈[0,1−Δ)m)−M⋅(1−ℙf0(𝒀∈[0,1−Δ)m)).\displaystyle\leq(\log M^{\prime})\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{0}}(\bm{Y}\in[0,1-\Delta)^{m})-M\cdot\bigl(1-{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{0}}(\bm{Y}\in[0,1-\Delta)^{m})\bigr).
(138)
Although the probability ℙf0(𝒀∈[0,1−Δ)m){\mathbb{P}}_{f_{0}}(\bm{Y}\in[0,1-\Delta)^{m}) depends on the shape of f0f_{0}, it is bounded away from both 00 and 11 when 0<Δ<10<\Delta<1. Hence, there exist positive constants cc and CC such that
𝔼f0⁡[hopt,M​(𝐘)]≤−Mc+C,\displaystyle\EB_{f_{0}}[h_{\text{opt},M}(\bm{Y})]\leq-Mc+C,
(139)
where cc and CC depend only on the fixed Δ\Delta used to define the score function hopt,Mh_{\text{opt},M}, but are independent of MM and 𝚫′\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}, the latter introduced in (II).
Analysis of Term (II).
We now analyze term (II), which takes the form of an integral over [0,1]m[0,1]^{m}
𝔼f𝚫¯′⁡[exp⁡(−hopt,M​(𝐘))]=∫[0,1]mexp⁡(−[log⁡f𝚫¯​(𝐲)f0​(𝐲)][−M,M])​f𝚫¯′​(𝐲)​𝑑𝐲,\displaystyle\EB_{f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}}[\exp(-h_{\text{opt},M}(\bm{Y}))]=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\exp\left(-\left[\log\frac{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})}{f_{0}(\bm{y})}\right]_{[-M,M]}\right)f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y},
where 𝚫¯=(Δ,…,Δ)\bar{\bm{\Delta}}=(\Delta,\ldots,\Delta) and 𝚫¯′=(Δ1′,…,Δm′)∈ℝm\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}=(\Delta_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\Delta_{m}^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}.
By Lemma C.4, the PDF f𝚫¯′f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}} vanishes outside the set ∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δℓ′)\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-\Delta_{\ell}^{\prime}). Hence, the domain of integration can be restricted to this support without altering the integral:
𝔼f𝚫¯′[exp(−hopt,M(𝐘))]=∫∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ′ℓ)exp(−[logf𝚫¯​(𝐲)f0​(𝐲)][−M,M])f𝚫¯′(𝐲)d𝐲.\displaystyle\EB_{f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}}[\exp(-h_{\text{opt},M}(\bm{Y}))]=\int_{\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-\Delta^{\prime}_{\ell})}\exp\left(-\left[\log\frac{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})}{f_{0}(\bm{y})}\right]_{[-M,M]}\right)f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y}.
From the analysis of term (I), we know that log⁡f𝚫¯​(𝒚)f0​(𝒚)\log\tfrac{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})}{f_{0}(\bm{y})} never exceeds MM once M≥|log⁡M′|M\geq|\log M^{\prime}|. Therefore, the clipping interval [−M,M][-M,M] can safely be replaced by [−M,∞)[-M,\infty). This yields
𝔼f𝚫¯′⁡[exp⁡(−hopt,M​(𝐘))]\displaystyle\EB_{f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}}[\exp(-h_{\text{opt},M}(\bm{Y}))]
=∫∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ′ℓ)exp(−[logf𝚫¯​(𝒚)f0​(𝒚)][−M,∞))f𝚫¯′(𝒚)d𝒚\displaystyle=\int_{\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-\Delta^{\prime}_{\ell})}\exp\left(-\left[\log\frac{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})}{f_{0}(\bm{y})}\right]_{[-M,\infty)}\right)f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y}
≤(a)∫∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ′ℓ)exp(−logf𝚫¯​(𝒚)f0​(𝒚))f𝚫¯′(𝒚)d𝒚\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\int_{\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-\Delta^{\prime}_{\ell})}\exp\left(-\log\frac{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})}{f_{0}(\bm{y})}\right)f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y}
=∫∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ′ℓ)f𝚫¯′​(𝒚)f𝚫¯​(𝒚)f0(𝒚)d𝒚,\displaystyle=\int_{\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-\Delta^{\prime}_{\ell})}\frac{f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})}{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})}f_{0}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y},
≤(b)∫∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ′ℓ)f0(𝒚)d𝒚⋅R≤R,\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\int_{\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-\Delta^{\prime}_{\ell})}f_{0}(\bm{y})\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{y}\cdot R\leq R,
(140)
where (a) holds because removing the lower clipping at −M-M can only increase the integral, and (b) follows from the uniform boundedness of f𝚫¯′​(𝒚)f𝚫¯​(𝒚)\tfrac{f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})}{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})} established in Lemma C.5.
Lemma C.5 (Uniformly bounded density ratio).
There exists a constant R>0R>0, independent of MM and 𝚫¯′\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime}, such that
supΔ≤𝚫¯′≤1−δsup𝒚∈∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ′ℓ)|f𝚫¯′​(𝒚)f𝚫¯​(𝒚)|≤R.\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime}\leq 1-\delta}\;\sup_{\bm{y}\in\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-\Delta^{\prime}_{\ell})}\left|\frac{f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})}{f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})}\right|\;\leq\;R.
Combining (137), (139), and (140), we conclude that there exist positive constants c,C,R>0c,C,R>0, independent of 𝚫¯′\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime} and MM, such that
L′​(hopt,M,𝚫¯′)≤−M​c+(C+R).L^{\prime}(h_{\text{opt},M},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime})\leq-Mc+(C+R).
Taking the supremum over 𝚫¯′\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime} and then letting M→∞M\to\infty gives
lim infM→∞supΔ≤𝚫¯′≤1−δL′​(hopt,M,𝚫¯′)=−∞.\liminf_{M\to\infty}\;\sup_{\Delta\leq\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime}\leq 1-\delta}L^{\prime}(h_{\text{opt},M},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime})=-\infty.
∎
Finally, we provide the proof of Lemma C.5 below.
Proof of Lemma C.5.
Fix any 𝒚=(y1,…,ym)∈∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δℓ′)\bm{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-\Delta_{\ell}^{\prime}).
Without loss of generality, assume y1y_{1} is the largest coordinate of 𝒚\bm{y}.
By definition, we have y1<1−Δ1′≤1−Δy_{1}<1-\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\leq 1-\Delta.
We then construct the auxiliary vector 𝒚′=(1−Δ1′,y2,…,ym)\bm{y}^{\prime}=(1-\Delta_{1}^{\prime},y_{2},\ldots,y_{m}), which differs from 𝒚\bm{y} only in its largest entry.
By the Lipschitz continuity in Lemma C.4, we know f𝚫¯′​(𝒚′)=0f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y}^{\prime})=0, and
|f𝚫¯′​(𝒚)|≤|f𝚫¯′​(𝒚)−f𝚫¯′​(𝒚′)|≤C⋅‖𝒚−𝒚′‖≤C⋅(1−Δ−maxℓ∈[m]⁡yℓ).|f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})|\leq|f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})-f_{\bar{\bm{\Delta}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y}^{\prime})|\leq C\cdot\|\bm{y}-\bm{y}^{\prime}\|\leq C\cdot\left(1-\Delta-\max_{\ell\in[m]}y_{\ell}\right).
(141)
where the constant CC, given in Lemma C.4, is independent of MM and of 𝚫¯′\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime}.
On the other hand, Theorem 5.1 gives
f𝚫¯​(𝒚)\displaystyle f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})
=Im​(𝚫¯)−1​∑𝝈∈{−1,1}m(B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)−A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚))+\displaystyle=I_{m}(\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}})^{-1}\sum_{\bm{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{m}}\left(B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})-A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})\right)_{+}
≥(a)​(B𝝈′𝚫¯′​(𝒚)−A𝝈′𝚫¯​(𝒚))+​=(b)​1−maxℓ∈[m]⁡yℓ1−Δ,\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}\left(B_{\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}^{\prime}}(\bm{y})-A_{\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})\right)_{+}\overset{(b)}{=}1-\frac{\max_{\ell\in[m]}y_{\ell}}{1-\Delta},
(142)
where (a) uses the fact that Im​(𝚫¯)≤1I_{m}(\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}})\leq 1 (since it is a probability) and keeps only the term with 𝝈′=(−1,…,−1)\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}=(-1,\ldots,-1), while (b) follows directly from the definitions of B𝝈′𝚫¯​(𝒚)B_{\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y}) and A𝝈′𝚫¯​(𝒚)A_{\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y}).
Combining (141) and (142) completes the proof with R=CR=C.
∎
C.9 Proof of Lemma C.4
Proof of Lemma C.4.
From Theorem 5.1, we know that
f𝚫¯​(𝒚)=1Im​(𝚫¯)​∑𝝈∈{−1,1}m(B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)−A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚))+,\displaystyle f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})=\frac{1}{I_{m}(\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}})}\sum_{\bm{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{m}}\left(B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})-A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})\right)_{+},
(143)
where for each sign vector 𝝈=(σ1,…,σmk)∈{−1,1}mk\bm{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{m_{k}})\in\{-1,1\}^{m_{k}} and input 𝒚=(y1,…,ym)\bm{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}),
L𝝈​(𝒚)\displaystyle L_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})
:=maxℓ∈[m]⁡(−σℓ​yℓ),\displaystyle:=\max_{\ell\in[m]}(-\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell}),
U𝝈​(𝒚)\displaystyle U_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})
:=minℓ∈[m]⁡(1−σℓ​yℓ),\displaystyle:=\min_{\ell\in[m]}(1-\sigma_{\ell}y_{\ell}),
Y𝝈+​(𝒚)\displaystyle Y^{+}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})
:=(maxℓ:σℓ=1Δ¯ℓ1−Δ¯ℓ⋅yℓ)+,\displaystyle:=\left(\max_{\ell:\sigma_{\ell}=1}\frac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}\cdot y_{\ell}\right)_{+},
Y𝝈−​(𝒚)\displaystyle Y^{-}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})
:=(maxℓ:σℓ=−1Δ¯ℓ1−Δ¯ℓ⋅yℓ)+,\displaystyle:=\left(\max_{\ell:\sigma_{\ell}=-1}\frac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}\cdot y_{\ell}\right)_{+},
A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)\displaystyle A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})
:=max⁡{L𝝈​(𝒚),Y𝝈+​(𝒚)},\displaystyle:=\max\left\{L_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y}),\ Y^{+}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\right\},
B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)\displaystyle B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})
:=min⁡{U𝝈​(𝒚), 1−Y𝝈−​(𝒚)},\displaystyle:=\min\left\{U_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y}),\ 1-Y^{-}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\right\},
with (x)+:=max⁡(x,0)(x)_{+}:=\max(x,0), and the normalization constant Im​(𝚫¯k)I_{m}(\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}_{k}) is given by
Im(𝚫¯):=∫[0,1]m(minℓ∈[m]{1−Δ¯k,ℓ+Δ¯k,ℓxℓ}−maxℓ∈[m]{Δ¯k,ℓxℓ})+dx1⋯dxm.I_{m}(\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}):=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\left(\min_{\ell\in[m]}\{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{k,\ell}+{\bar{\Delta}}_{k,\ell}x_{\ell}\}-\max_{\ell\in[m]}\{{\bar{\Delta}}_{k,\ell}x_{\ell}\}\right)_{+}{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m}.
Part 1: Support of f𝚫¯f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}.
We first show that f𝚫¯​(𝒚)=0f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})=0 whenever 𝒚∉∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ¯ℓ)\bm{y}\notin\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}).
Take any 𝒚∈[0,1]m\bm{y}\in[0,1]^{m} with yℓ≥1−Δ¯ℓy_{\ell}\geq 1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell} for some index ℓ\ell. Consider two cases depending on σℓ\sigma_{\ell}:
1.
If σℓ=1\sigma_{\ell}=1, then
Y𝝈+​(𝒚)≥Δ¯ℓ1−Δ¯ℓ​(1−Δ¯ℓ)=Δ¯ℓ,U𝝈​(𝒚)≤ 1−(1−Δ¯ℓ)=Δ¯ℓ.Y^{+}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\;\geq\;\tfrac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}(1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell})={\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell},\quad U_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\;\leq\;1-(1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell})={\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}.
Hence, A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)≥Δ¯ℓA_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})\geq{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell} and
B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)≤Δ¯ℓB_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})\leq{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}, so that
(B𝝈𝚫¯−A𝝈𝚫¯)+=0(B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}-A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}})_{+}=0.
2.
If σℓ=−1\sigma_{\ell}=-1, then
Y𝝈−​(𝒚)≥Δ¯ℓ1−Δ¯ℓ​(1−Δ¯ℓ)=Δ¯ℓ,L𝝈​(𝒚)≥ 1−Δ¯ℓ.Y^{-}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\;\geq\;\tfrac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}(1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell})={\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell},\quad L_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\;\geq\;1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}.
Consequently, A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)≥1−Δ¯ℓA_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})\geq 1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell} while
B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)≤1−Δ¯ℓB_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})\leq 1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}, so again
(B𝝈𝚫¯−A𝝈𝚫¯)+=0(B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}-A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}})_{+}=0.
Since this holds for every 𝝈\bm{\sigma}, the entire sum vanishes and f𝚫¯​(𝒚)=0f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})=0.
Thus the support is contained in ∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ¯ℓ)\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}).
Conversely, let 𝒚∈∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ¯ℓ−ε]\bm{y}\in\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}-{\varepsilon}] for any small ε>0{\varepsilon}>0.
Take 𝝈=(1,…,1)\bm{\sigma}=(1,\ldots,1). Then
L𝝈​(𝒚)≤0,U𝝈​(𝒚)≥Δ¯ℓ+ε​for all ​ℓ,Y𝝈−​(𝒚)=0,L_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\leq 0,\quad U_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\geq{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\varepsilon}\ \text{for all }\ell,\quad Y^{-}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})=0,
and
Y𝝈+​(𝒚)≤maxℓ⁡Δ¯ℓ1−Δ¯ℓ​(1−Δ¯ℓ−ε)≤maxℓ⁡Δ¯ℓ−minℓ⁡Δ¯ℓ1−Δ¯ℓ​ε.Y^{+}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\leq\max_{\ell}\tfrac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}(1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}-{\varepsilon})\leq\max_{\ell}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}-\min_{\ell}\tfrac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{\varepsilon}.
Hence,
A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)=Y𝝈+​(𝒚)≤maxℓ⁡Δ¯ℓ−minℓ⁡Δ¯ℓ1−Δ¯ℓ​ε,B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)=U𝝈​(𝒚)≥maxℓ⁡Δ¯ℓ+ε.A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})=Y^{+}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\leq\max_{\ell}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}-\min_{\ell}\tfrac{{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}{\varepsilon},\quad B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})=U_{\bm{\sigma}}(\bm{y})\geq\max_{\ell}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}+{\varepsilon}.
By Assumption 5.1, 0<Δ¯ℓ<10<{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}<1, so the gap
B𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)−A𝝈𝚫¯​(𝒚)≥ε1−maxℓ⁡Δ¯ℓ>0.B_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})-A_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})\;\geq\;\frac{{\varepsilon}}{1-\max_{\ell}{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}}>0.
Thus, at least one term in the summation is strictly positive, and
f𝚫¯​(𝒚)>0f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y})>0.
This proves that f𝚫¯f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}} is strictly positive on
∏ℓ=1m[0,1−Δ¯ℓ)\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}[0,1-{\bar{\Delta}}_{\ell}) and zero elsewhere.
Part 2: Lipschitz continuity.
The joint density f𝚫¯​(𝒚)f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}}(\bm{y}) is uniformly continuous in
(𝒚,𝚫¯)(\bm{y},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}) on [0,1]m×[0,1−δ]m[0,1]^{m}\times[0,1-\delta]^{m}, since it is given by a finite sum of continuous functions on a compact domain. To strengthen this to Lipschitz continuity, recall from Theorem 5.1 that each summand in the representation of f𝚫¯f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}} is constructed from a finite combination of linear functions, maxima, minima, and positive-part operators. Each of these building blocks is Lipschitz continuous with constants depending only on (m,δ)(m,\delta), and finite maxima/minima of Lipschitz functions remain Lipschitz with constants given by the maximum of the individual constants.
Therefore, every summand is Lipschitz continuous with respect to (𝒚,𝚫¯)(\bm{y},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}), uniformly on the domain [0,1]m×[0,1−δ]m[0,1]^{m}\times[0,1-\delta]^{m}. Since f𝚫¯f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}} is a finite sum of such summands, it follows that f𝚫¯f_{\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}} itself is Lipschitz continuous, with a constant depending only on (m,δ)(m,\delta), but independent of (𝒚,𝚫¯)(\bm{y},\bm{{\bar{\Delta}}}).
∎
C.10 Auxiliary Lemmas
Lemma C.6.
Let
Im(Δ)=∫[0,1]m(1−Δ−ΔD(x1,…,xm))+dx1⋯dxm,D(x1,…,xm)=max1≤i≤mxi−min1≤i≤mxi.I_{m}(\Delta)=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\bigl(1-\Delta-\Delta\,D(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\bigr)_{+}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{m},\quad D(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})=\max_{1\leq i\leq m}x_{i}-\min_{1\leq i\leq m}x_{i}.
Then for 0≤Δ<10\leq\Delta<1, we have the closed form
Im​(Δ)={1−2​mm+1​Δ,0≤Δ≤12,(1−ΔΔ)m​[ 1−2​m​(1−Δ)m+1],12<Δ<1.I_{m}(\Delta)=\begin{cases}1-\dfrac{2m}{m+1}\,\Delta,&0\leq\Delta\leq\tfrac{1}{2},\\[10.00002pt]
\displaystyle\left(\frac{1-\Delta}{\Delta}\right)^{m}\left[\,1-\frac{2m(1-\Delta)}{m+1}\right],&\tfrac{1}{2}<\Delta<1.\end{cases}
Moreover, Im​(Δ)I_{m}(\Delta) is uniformly continuous on [0,1−δ][0,1-\delta] for any given δ∈(0,1)\delta\in(0,1).
Proof of Lemma C.6.
Let X1,…,XmX_{1},\ldots,X_{m} be i.i.d. Unif⁡(0,1)\mathrm{Unif}(0,1). Then the target quantity can be expressed as Im​(Δ)=𝔼⁡[(1−Δ−Δ​D)+]I_{m}(\Delta)=\EB\left[\left(1-\Delta-\Delta\,D\right)_{+}\right], where D=maxi⁡Xi−mini⁡Xi∈[0,1]D=\max_{i}X_{i}-\min_{i}X_{i}\in[0,1]. The density of DD is given by fD​(r)=m⁡(m−1)​rm−2​(1−r)f_{D}(r)=m(m-1)\,r^{m-2}(1-r) for r∈[0,1]r\in[0,1], as stated in formula (2.5.15) of [35].
Hence,
Im​(Δ)=∫01(1−Δ−Δ​r)+​fD​(r)​𝑑r=∫0r0(1−Δ−Δ​r)​m​(m−1)​rm−2​(1−r)​𝑑r,I_{m}(\Delta)=\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\Delta-\Delta r\right)_{+}f_{D}(r)\,{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{0}^{r_{0}}(1-\Delta-\Delta r)\,m(m-1)\,r^{m-2}(1-r)\,{\mathrm{d}}r,
where r0=(1−Δ)/Δr_{0}=(1-\Delta)/\Delta, since the integrand becomes zero for r>r0r>r_{0}.
Case 1: Δ≤12\Delta\leq\tfrac{1}{2}. Then r0≥1r_{0}\geq 1, so the (⋅)+(\cdot)_{+} operator has no effect over r∈[0,1]r\in[0,1]. Therefore,
Im​(Δ)=∫01((1−Δ)−Δ​r)​m​(m−1)​rm−2​(1−r)​𝑑r.I_{m}(\Delta)=\int_{0}^{1}\left((1-\Delta)-\Delta r\right)\,m(m-1)\,r^{m-2}(1-r)\,dr.
This evaluates to
Im​(Δ)=(1−Δ)−Δ⋅m−1m+1=1−2​mm+1​Δ.I_{m}(\Delta)=(1-\Delta)-\Delta\cdot\frac{m-1}{m+1}=1-\frac{2m}{m+1}\,\Delta.
Case 2: Δ>12\Delta>\tfrac{1}{2}. Then r0<1r_{0}<1, and the integral becomes
Im​(Δ)=m⁡(m−1)​∫0r0(1−Δ−Δ​r)​rm−2​(1−r)​𝑑r.I_{m}(\Delta)=m(m-1)\int_{0}^{r_{0}}(1-\Delta-\Delta r)\,r^{m-2}(1-r)\,dr.
Let
A=∫0r0rm−2​(1−r)​𝑑r=r0m−1m−1−r0mm,B=∫0r0rm−1​(1−r)​𝑑r=r0mm−r0m+1m+1.A=\int_{0}^{r_{0}}r^{m-2}(1-r)\,dr=\frac{r_{0}^{m-1}}{m-1}-\frac{r_{0}^{m}}{m},\quad B=\int_{0}^{r_{0}}r^{m-1}(1-r)\,dr=\frac{r_{0}^{m}}{m}-\frac{r_{0}^{m+1}}{m+1}.
Then we have
Im​(Δ)=m⁡(m−1)​[(1−Δ)​A−Δ​B].I_{m}(\Delta)=m(m-1)\left[(1-\Delta)A-\Delta B\right].
Substituting r0=(1−Δ)/Δr_{0}=(1-\Delta)/\Delta and simplifying gives
Im​(Δ)=(1−ΔΔ)m​[1−2​m​(1−Δ)m+1].I_{m}(\Delta)=\left(\frac{1-\Delta}{\Delta}\right)^{m}\left[1-\frac{2m(1-\Delta)}{m+1}\right].
The uniform continuity of Im​(Δ)I_{m}(\Delta) over [0,1−δ][0,1-\delta] follows directly from the smoothness of the integrand and the compactness of the domain. This concludes the proof.
∎
Lemma C.7 ([2], Theorem 2.1).
Let {ai,j}1≤i,j≤|𝒲|\{a_{i,j}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq|{{\mathcal{W}}}|} be a collection of real numbers, and let π\pi be a uniformly random permutation on 𝒲{{\mathcal{W}}}. Define
Z=∑j=1|𝒲|aj,π⁡(j).Z\;=\;\sum_{j=1}^{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}a_{j,\pi(j)}.
Then for all x>0x>0,
ℙ⁡(|Z−𝔼⁡[Z]|≥ 2​ 2​(1|𝒲|​∑i,j=1|𝒲|ai,j2)​x+ 2​max1≤i,j≤|𝒲|​|ai,j|​x)≤ 16​e1/16​exp⁡(−x16).{\mathbb{P}}\left(\bigl|Z-\EB[Z]\bigr|\;\geq\;2\sqrt{\,2\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}\sum_{i,j=1}^{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}a_{i,j}^{2}\Bigr)\,x\;+\;2\max_{1\leq i,j\leq|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}|a_{i,j}|\;x}\right)\;\leq\;16\,e^{1/16}\,\exp\Bigl(-\tfrac{x}{16}\Bigr).
(15)
Lemma C.8.
The maximum function max:[0,1]m→[0,1]\max:[0,1]^{m}\to[0,1] is Lipschitz continuous with constant 11 with respect to the L∞L^{\infty} norm. That is, for any x,y∈[0,1]mx,y\in[0,1]^{m}, we have
|max⁡(x1,…,xm)−max⁡(y1,…,ym)|≤maxi=1,…,m⁡|xi−yi|.|\max(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})-\max(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})|\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}|x_{i}-y_{i}|.
(144)
Similarly, the minimum function min:[0,1]m→[0,1]\min:[0,1]^{m}\to[0,1] is also Lipschitz continuous with constant 11 under the L∞L^{\infty} norm:
|min⁡(x1,…,xm)−min⁡(y1,…,ym)|≤maxi=1,…,m⁡|xi−yi|.|\min(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})-\min(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})|\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}|x_{i}-y_{i}|.
(145)
Proof of Lemma C.8.
Without loss of generality, assume max⁡(x1,…,xm)=xi0≥max⁡(y1,…,ym)=yj0\max(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})=x_{i_{0}}\geq\max(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})=y_{j_{0}} for some indices i0,j0∈[m]i_{0},j_{0}\in[m]. Since yj0≥yi0y_{j_{0}}\geq y_{i_{0}} (as yj0y_{j_{0}} is the maximum of yy), we have
|max⁡(x1,…,xm)−max⁡(y1,…,ym)|\displaystyle|\max(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})-\max(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})|
=xi0−yj0\displaystyle=x_{i_{0}}-y_{j_{0}}
(146)
≤xi0−yi0≤maxi=1,…,m⁡|xi−yi|.\displaystyle\leq x_{i_{0}}-y_{i_{0}}\leq\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}|x_{i}-y_{i}|.
(147)
This proves the Lipschitz continuity of the maximum function. The result for the minimum follows by noting that
min⁡(x1,…,xm)=−max⁡(−x1,…,−xm),\min(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})=-\max(-x_{1},\ldots,-x_{m}),
and applying the same argument to −x-x and −y-y.
∎
Lemma C.9.
Let X1,…,XmX_{1},\dots,X_{m} be independent Unif⁡(0,1)\mathrm{Unif}(0,1) random variables, and conditionally on (X1,…,Xm)=𝐱:=(x1,…,xm)(X_{1},\dots,X_{m})=\bm{x}:=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}), let
U∼Unif⁡[a⁡(𝒙),b⁡(𝒙)],U\sim\mathrm{Unif}\bigl[a(\bm{x}),\,b(\bm{x})\bigr],
where a⁡(𝐱)=max1≤ℓ≤m⁡{Δℓ​xℓ}a(\bm{x})=\max_{1\leq\ell\leq m}\{\Delta_{\ell}x_{\ell}\} and b⁡(𝐱)=min1≤ℓ≤m⁡{Δℓ​xℓ+1−Δℓ}b(\bm{x})=\min_{1\leq\ell\leq m}\{\Delta_{\ell}x_{\ell}+1-\Delta_{\ell}\} with the convention that UU has no mass if a⁡(x)≥b⁡(x)a(x)\geq b(x).
With 𝚫¯=(Δ¯1,…,Δ¯m)\bar{\bm{\Delta}}=({\bar{\Delta}}_{1},\ldots,{\bar{\Delta}}_{m}), define
Im​(𝚫¯)=∫[0,1]m(b⁡(x)−a⁡(x))+​𝑑x.I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}})=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\bigl(b(x)-a(x)\bigr)_{+}\,{\mathrm{d}}x.
Then for any measurable function J:[0,1]m+1→[0,∞)J\colon[0,1]^{m+1}\to[0,\infty), we have
𝔼⁡[J⁡(U,X1,…,Xm)]=1Im​(𝚫¯)​∫[0,1]m∫a⁡(x)b⁡(x)J⁡(u,x1,…,xm)​du⋅𝟏a⁡(𝐱)<b⁡(𝐱)​𝑑𝐱,\displaystyle\EB\bigl[J(U,X_{1},\dots,X_{m})\bigr]=\frac{1}{I_{m}(\bar{\bm{\Delta}})}\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\int_{a(x)}^{b(x)}J\bigl(u,x_{1},\dots,x_{m}\bigr){\mathrm{d}}u\,\cdot\mathbf{1}_{a(\bm{x})<b(\bm{x})}\,{\mathrm{d}}\bm{x},
(148)
and hence the right-hand side defines the joint law of (U,X1,…,Xm)(U,X_{1},\dots,X_{m}).
Proof of Lemma C.9.
This follows directly from the law of total expectation and the conditional definition of UU, so we omit the details.
∎
Lemma C.10 (Integral approximation error).
Let J:[0,1]m+1→ℝJ:[0,1]^{m+1}\to\mathbb{R} be a 1-Lipschitz function.
Let aπa_{\pi} and bπb_{\pi} be random variables depending on a random variable π\pi, and let a¯,b¯∈[0,1]\bar{a},\bar{b}\in[0,1] be deterministic values.
Then, for any fixed fractions {xℓ}ℓ=1m\{x_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m}, the following bound holds:
|𝔼π[∫aπbπJ(u,x1,…,xm)𝟏{bπ≥aπ}du]−∫a¯b¯J(u,x1,…,xm)𝟏{b¯≥a¯}du|\displaystyle\left|\EB_{\pi}\left[\int_{a_{\pi}}^{b_{\pi}}J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}){\bf 1}_{\{b_{\pi}\geq a_{\pi}\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right]-\int_{\bar{a}}^{\bar{b}}J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}){\bf 1}_{\{\bar{b}\geq\bar{a}\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right|
≤2​‖J‖∞⋅𝔼π⁡[|aπ−a¯|+|bπ−b¯|].\displaystyle\leq 2\|J\|_{\infty}\cdot\EB_{\pi}\left[|a_{\pi}-\bar{a}|+|b_{\pi}-\bar{b}|\right].
(149)
where ‖J‖∞:=max(u,x1,…,xm)∈[0,1]m+1⁡|J⁡(u,x1,…,xm)|\|J\|_{\infty}:=\max\limits_{(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m+1}}|J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})| denotes the bound on JJ.
Proof of Lemma C.10.
Since the values {xℓ}ℓ=1m\{x_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{m} are fixed, define the simplified function J⁡(u):=J⁡(u,x1,…,xm)J(u):=J(u,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}).
We decompose JJ into its positive and negative parts:
J=J+−J−,whereJ+​(u):=max⁡{J⁡(u),0},J−​(u):=max⁡{−J⁡(u),0}.J=J_{+}-J_{-},\quad\text{where}\quad J_{+}(u):=\max\{J(u),0\},\quad J_{-}(u):=\max\{-J(u),0\}.
Both J+J_{+} and J−J_{-} are non-negative and 1-Lipschitz, with ‖J+‖∞,‖J−‖∞≤‖J‖∞\|J_{+}\|_{\infty},\|J_{-}\|_{\infty}\leq\|J\|_{\infty}.
Note that for any non-negative function ff, we have:
(∫abf(u)du)𝟏{b≥a}=(∫abf(u)du)+.\left(\int_{a}^{b}f(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right){\bf 1}_{\{b\geq a\}}=\left(\int_{a}^{b}f(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)_{+}.
Applying this to J+J_{+} and J−J_{-}, we write:
∫aπbπJ(u)du⋅𝟏{bπ≥aπ}−∫a¯b¯J(u)du⋅𝟏{b¯≥a¯}\displaystyle\int_{a_{\pi}}^{b_{\pi}}J(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{b_{\pi}\geq a_{\pi}\}}-\int_{\bar{a}}^{\bar{b}}J(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\bar{b}\geq\bar{a}\}}
=(∫aπbπJ+​(u)​𝑑u)+−(∫a¯b¯J+​(u)​𝑑u)+−[(∫aπbπJ−​(u)​𝑑u)+−(∫a¯b¯J−​(u)​𝑑u)+].\displaystyle=\left(\int_{a_{\pi}}^{b_{\pi}}J_{+}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)_{+}-\left(\int_{\bar{a}}^{\bar{b}}J_{+}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)_{+}-\left[\left(\int_{a_{\pi}}^{b_{\pi}}J_{-}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)_{+}-\left(\int_{\bar{a}}^{\bar{b}}J_{-}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)_{+}\right].
Applying the triangle inequality and the fact that (⋅)+(\cdot)_{+} is 1-Lipschitz, we obtain:
|∫aπbπJ(u)du⋅𝟏{bπ≥aπ}−∫a¯b¯J(u)du⋅𝟏{b¯≥a¯}|\displaystyle\left|\int_{a_{\pi}}^{b_{\pi}}J(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{b_{\pi}\geq a_{\pi}\}}-\int_{\bar{a}}^{\bar{b}}J(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{\bar{b}\geq\bar{a}\}}\right|
≤|(∫aπbπJ+​(u)​𝑑u)−(∫a¯b¯J+​(u)​𝑑u)|+|(∫aπbπJ−​(u)​𝑑u)−(∫a¯b¯J−​(u)​𝑑u)|\displaystyle\leq\left|\left(\int_{a_{\pi}}^{b_{\pi}}J_{+}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)-\left(\int_{\bar{a}}^{\bar{b}}J_{+}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)\right|+\left|\left(\int_{a_{\pi}}^{b_{\pi}}J_{-}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)-\left(\int_{\bar{a}}^{\bar{b}}J_{-}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}u\right)\right|
≤2​‖J‖∞⋅(|aπ−a¯|+|bπ−b¯|).\displaystyle\leq 2\|J\|_{\infty}\cdot(|a_{\pi}-\bar{a}|+|b_{\pi}-\bar{b}|).
Taking expectation over π\pi completes the proof.
∎
Appendix D Details of Simulation Study
Choice of pseudorandom variable.
We use a context window of size m=7m=7, so the pseudorandom variable ζt=𝒜(s(t−m):(t−1),𝙺𝚎𝚢)\zeta_{t}={\mathcal{A}}(s_{(t-m):(t-1)},{\mathtt{Key}}) depends on the preceding mm tokens. With such a relatively large mm, nearly all pseudorandom collisions stem from our generation mechanism. In practice, at each step tt, the hash function 𝒜{\mathcal{A}} takes as input the mm most recent tokens concatenated with the key 𝙺𝚎𝚢{\mathtt{Key}}, producing a hash value that serves as a random seed. This seed is then passed to a pseudorandom number generator, for which we use the PCG-64 generator [38], the default in Python’s NumPy package [17].
Computation of critical values.
For the Gumbel-max watermark under score functions harsh_{\mathrm{ars}} and hlogh_{\mathrm{log}}, the sum of score values follows a gamma distribution. In this case, the critical values can be obtained directly from the gamma (1−α)(1-\alpha) quantile. For other score functions, the exact distribution of the score sum is generally unavailable, so we rely on Monte Carlo simulation.
Concretely, for each nn, we generate nn i.i.d. samples of the corresponding pivotal statistic YY from μ0\mu_{0} and compute ∑𝒱∈Πh⁡(Y𝒱)\sum_{{\mathcal{V}}\in\Pi}h(Y_{{\mathcal{V}}}) for the score function hh. This procedure is repeated 4000 times, and the empirical (1−α)(1-\alpha) quantile of these values serves as an estimate.
Additional results.
Figure 6 reports Type II errors for other values of Δmax\Delta_{\max}, showing patterns consistent with those in Section 6.1.
Figure 6: Type II errors on synthetic datasets for the Gumbel-max watermark (left) and the inverse-transform watermark (right), with results shown for Δmax∈{0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7}\Delta_{\max}\in\{0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7\} from top to bottom.
Appendix E Details for Real-World Examples
Detailed experimental setup.
In our empirical analysis of the detection performance of different watermark detection methods, we focus on the OPT-1.3B model [52].
We evaluate Type I errors using 2000 human-written samples from the C4 news-like dataset [42].
Specifically, for each human-written document in the dataset, we select it if and only if it contains at least 520 tokens, and we take the last 500 tokens as the initial prompt.
For each selected sample, we apply a hash function 𝒜{\mathcal{A}} to compute the corresponding sequence of pseudorandom variables.
This procedure is repeated until we collect 2000 sequences, each containing 500 pivotal statistics.
To assess Type II errors, we randomly sample prompts from the same dataset.
We enforce a minimum prompt length of 50 tokens in all experiments and skip any document shorter than this threshold.
Each 50-token prompt is then fed into the model, which generates an additional 800 tokens.
Since 800 tokens are sufficiently long, we retain the generated text only if it contains at least 300 unique minimal units; otherwise, the generation is discarded.
Following this procedure, we collect 200 generated sequences, each consisting of at least 300 minimal units.
The temperature parameter controls the randomness of LLM generation.
Let 𝑳=(L1,…,L|𝒲|)\bm{L}=(L_{1},\ldots,L_{|{{\mathcal{W}}}|}) denote the model’s logit vector over the vocabulary 𝒲{{\mathcal{W}}}.
The temperature β\beta rescales this vector to obtain the token distribution 𝑷\bm{P},
Pw=exp⁡(Lw/β)∑w′∈𝒲exp⁡(Lw′/β).P_{{w}}=\frac{\exp(L_{{w}}/\beta)}{\sum_{{w}^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{W}}}}\exp(L_{{w}^{\prime}}/\beta)}.
A smaller β\beta yields a more deterministic distribution.
To ensure a clear comparison across methods, we adopt relatively low temperatures: β=0.3\beta=0.3 for the Gumbel-max watermark and β=0.5\beta=0.5 for the inverse-transform watermark.
At higher temperatures (that is, more random generations), all detection methods tend to achieve nearly indistinguishable power within short text lengths.
Details of Figure 1.
We describe the experimental setup used to produce Figure 1.
The left panel quantifies the proportion of token repetitions in both human-written and watermarked texts.
•
For the human-written case, we extract sentences from the C4 news-like dataset [42]. Each sentence is tokenized using the OPT-1.3B decoder, and we retain those with at least 200 tokens, collecting 1000 sequences in total. For a given text window size m∈{2,3,…,10}m\in\{2,3,\ldots,10\}, we compute the proportion of repeated tokens within each sequence and report the average repetition rate across all sequences.
•
For the watermarked case, we sample 1000 prompts, each containing at least 50 tokens, and generate the following 200 tokens using the Gumbel-max watermark with temperature 1. When generating each text, we specify a window size mm, which is used to compute the pseudorandom variables. We then measure the repetition rate for each generation and report the final value as the average across all 1000 samples.
The right panel of Figure 1 demonstrates that classic detection methods fail to control Type I error. To verify this, we generate 1000 sequences of 1000 tokens each using the OPT-1.3B model with temperature 0.1, without applying any watermarking. We then apply existing detection methods to these sequences and evaluate their empirical Type I error rates.
